  - *Checked.* `dotnet build`; `dotnet test` (698 pass, 29 new: recognition of every kind from small made-up files, family words and v-prediction markers, trigger words from the three samples' tag lists, damaged headers, a `.ckpt` held locked by another handle still reported as refused (so it was never opened), folder scan with misplaced and refused files, family defaults and their limits, settings round-trip with reset, prompt assembly, the database document holding no full path and surviving a portable move, the recommended downloads' saved names recognised as the right family; plus the real sample files when `DOGAI_TEST_IMAGE_MODEL` and `DOGAI_TEST_IMAGE_LORAS` are set, run and passed). **In the app** (throwaway data and models folders, the samples hard-linked in and the links removed afterwards; samples unchanged; the picture engine copy installed in step 2's walkthrough through `DOGAI_SD_SERVER`; graphics memory free, no chat model loaded): the checkpoint shown as "SDXL checkpoint, Illustrious" with Illustrious defaults; the three LoRAs with the trigger words above; a LoRA linked into the checkpoints folder shown with "move it to the loras folder"; a `.ckpt` shown as refused; a test picture drawn from the tab in 11.1 s (first, with loading), then with Maid_marian in 9.5 s, both looked at; a changed step count saved and shown as "your settings" after leaving and reopening the tab. The tab was shown in all three looks, dark and light, but not every part in every look; what was looked at: Workbench dark: the top with the lists, the "other files" (misplaced and refused), and both drawn pictures; Workbench light: the checkpoint card, before and after the fix; Fireside dark: the top and the checkpoint card, before and after the fix; Fireside light: the downloads, folder and engine box; Playhouse dark: the top and the LoRA card with its training tags; Playhouse light: the checkpoint card and the downloads. One layout flaw was found and fixed (a dropdown stretched beside a field that has a note under it). Going online and checking for the engine from the tab worked; it said the newest release's Windows files were not up yet, and that was true: release master-1098 came out at 09:33:04 UTC, its CUDA file finished uploading at 09:33:45 and the check ran at about 09:33:55 (both files are there now, under names the updater matches). Nothing was installed. The Engine tab seen without the test buttons. No warnings in the app log; no engine left running.
  - *Not checked.* **No download was made** (none asked for): a real download into the image folder and its "Installed" state are untested in the app. Open folder was not clicked (it opens File Explorer). **Cancel** on the test picture was not clicked (the same cancel as step 3's, which was tried). The **waiting** and **making room** messages were never on screen, because no chat model was loaded. The Install button on the Images tab was not seen (the check happened in the minutes when the newest release had no Windows files). No v-prediction checkpoint, separate VAE, LyCORIS, SD 1.5 or Flux file was tried for real (only made-up headers). The family defaults for NoobAI, Pony and SDXL were not drawn with. The tab with a chat model loaded (the coordinator's swap from this tab) was not tried; the coordinator is the same as in step 3. The checkpoint picker's choice is not used by anything else yet.
- **M9 step 3, sharing the graphics card (2026-11-14).** The chat model and the picture model now take turns on the graphics card by themselves. There is still nothing to draw with in the app (steps 4 to 8); what the owner can see is the chat model marked "set aside" after a picture and coming back with the next message.
  - *What exists.* `CardCoordinator` (Core/Images) is the picture engine the whole app gets (`IImageEngine`); the real `ImageServer` sits behind it, so no picture can be drawn without it. Before each picture it works out what the picture needs (`PictureMemory.Need`: the checkpoint file plus a quarter, the LoRAs' sizes, and 4 GB per million pixels of the largest pass, which gives 12.1 GB for Standard and 17.6 GB for Fine with the owner's checkpoint), measures the card (`CardMeter`: total and free memory, and what each engine holds), and decides: if the need, less what the picture model already holds, fits in free memory with 1 GB to spare, both stay; otherwise the chat model is **set aside**. A card that cannot be measured gets the swap.
  - *"Set aside"* is a new state of the chat engine (`EngineState.Resting`, `EngineManager.RestAsync`): its program ends and the memory comes back, but the model, its context size and its settings are remembered. Chat pages treat it as ready (`IsReady`), so the message box and every button work; the first thing that really asks the model (a reply, a prompt being measured) loads it again exactly as it was. It is **not** the owner's Unload: the owner's hold in `ModelLauncher` is untouched, a model the owner unloaded is never loaded by the coordinator, the owner's **Unload** on a model that is set aside forgets it for good, opening a page does not load a default over it, and the unload-when-unused timer leaves it alone (it holds no memory).
  - *One swap for a run of pictures.* After the picture the chat model is not loaded again. "Try again" and further pictures find nothing to move. The next message brings it back, and the picture model (which stays chosen) is let go first unless both are measured to fit.
  - *Nothing races.* One thing at a time changes what is on the card ("a turn"). A picture waits for a reply that is being written (the reply is never cut off) and for a chat model that is loading. **A chat request that arrives while a picture is drawing waits** for the picture, then for the model to load, and is then answered. Decided against a plain "try again later" message because a picture takes seconds, and a message that was already sent would otherwise sit unanswered until the owner pressed Answer; Stop still works while it waits. Loading a model by hand and benchmarking wait in the same way, and let the idle picture model go first, so that what fits is measured against a card without the picture model's 8 GB on it (otherwise part of a big chat model would be pushed off the card).
  - *Idle time of the picture model: 2 minutes* (was a 5-minute placeholder), counted from the end of the last picture and checked every 15 seconds. Why: keeping it costs 8 GB, a third of the card, and getting it back costs about 4 seconds; two minutes covers looking at a picture, changing the prompt and drawing again. The chat model never waits for this timer.
  - *Idle lock.* `AppLock` counts the picture engine's `IsBusy`, which is true from the moment a picture is asked for (waiting, making room, drawing) until it is finished or has failed.
  - *On screen (no more than needed to see it).* The model button says "· set aside" with an amber dot; the model picker says the model "was set aside to make room for pictures. It loads again by itself with your next message", with **Unload**; Workbench's status bar says the same; Models → Engine has a **Now** line for the picture engine. Two test buttons (**Draw a test picture**, **Draw a fine one**) appear on Models → Engine only when `DOGAI_TEST_IMAGE_MODEL` names a checkpoint; step 4's Images tab replaces them. The character editor's token count no longer asks a model that is set aside.
  - *Measured with the real engines* (RTX 4090, card otherwise at 0.8 to 1.2 GB; the owner's 27B model read where it is, the sample checkpoint read where it is, the trial's copy of the picture engine through `DOGAI_SD_SERVER`, nothing downloaded): the 27B at 8,192 context holds 17.0 to 17.2 GB, leaving 5.2 to 5.8 GB free. First picture: chat model set aside, picture drawn in 12.6 to 14.4 s (6.3 s without a swap); afterwards 8.5 GB in use (picture model 7.3 GB). Second picture: no swap, 6.3 s, card peaks at 12.1 GB in use. Next message: picture model let go, 27B loaded again in 30 s and answering after 31 to 37 s, back at 17.0 GB (whole, not squeezed). A Fine picture (832×1216 with hires fix ×1.5 comes out 1280×1856, not 1248×1824): swap again, 21 s, card peaks at 17.7 GB. With a 7 GB chat model (8.0 GB on the card) **both stay loaded**: no swap, card peaks at 20.1 GB, pictures in 6.5 s, the next reply starts at once. This is the test the trial did not run.
  - *Checked.* `dotnet build`; `dotnet test` (669 pass). New tests with fakes: both fit; do not fit; a run of pictures costs one swap and the next message brings the model back; the picture model stays when the returning model fits; Fine needs the swap when Standard does not; an unmeasured card; a model the owner unloaded stays unloaded (before a picture, and while set aside); a chat request during a picture waits and is answered, and can be stopped; a model loaded by hand during a picture waits and gets a free card; a picture waits for a reply and counts as busy all along; cancelling a waiting picture; one picture at a time; a bad request moves nothing; LoRAs count; the estimates against the trial's numbers. `CardSharingSmokeTests` runs the real engines when `DOGAI_TEST_MODEL`, `DOGAI_SD_SERVER` and `DOGAI_TEST_IMAGE_MODEL` are set; run with the 27B (swap) and the 7 GB model (both stay), including the owner's Unload while set aside. **In the app** (three walkthroughs on throwaway data folders, graphics memory sampled twice a second from outside): the 27B loaded and answered, picture, "try again", General showing "set aside", next message answered by the 27B, Fine picture; then the set-aside notice in all three looks, dark and light, with Characters, Story, Settings and Models opened and nothing coming back (no llama-server process), and the picture engine gone after its two idle minutes; then a locked data folder with a 20-second idle lock: it stayed open through a 17-second picture and locked within a second of the picture finishing ("nobody used the PC for 22 seconds").
  - *Not checked.* Only with fakes, because nothing in the app can do it yet: a picture asked for while a reply is being written, a chat request sent while a picture is drawing, a model loaded by hand during a picture. Not tried at all: **Stop** pressed while the model is coming back (by design the reply stops and the load carries on); the owner's **Unload** clicked in the app while set aside (the real-engines test does it through the same code); a model that cannot come back (file gone, or another program has taken the card: the engine's own error would show); a benchmark with the picture model loaded; group chats, stories and the document search model alongside pictures; LoRAs in a real picture through the coordinator (they are counted by file size, not measured); picture sizes other than 832×1216 (the "4 GB per million pixels" is drawn from two measurements); the portable build. **Known limit:** when another program holds a lot of the card (a game, Ollama with a model loaded), setting the chat model aside may not be enough; the picture is drawn anyway and will be slow or fail, with no plain message yet. Models' hardware panel is a snapshot from when the page opened (**Measure again**), so it can show the chat model's memory after it was set aside.
  - *For later steps.* Draw only through `IImageEngine` from the app's services (never `ImageServer`), and after the chat model's prompt job has ended, not from inside a streaming request: a picture that needs the room waits for the request to finish. Show the two extra progress stages (`CardCoordinator.Waiting`, `MakingRoom`). Use `EngineManager.IsLoaded`, not `IsReady`, for anything passive that should not bring the model back (counting tokens while typing). Anything new that loads a chat model goes through `ModelLauncher.LoadAsync` or takes a turn (`EngineManager.TakeCardTurnAsync`) and keeps it until the load ends.
- **M9 step 2, the picture engine inside DOG.ai (2026-11-14).** DOG.ai can now install, start, drive and stop stable-diffusion.cpp's `sd-server`. There is nothing to draw with in the app yet (steps 3 to 8); the only thing the owner sees is the engine's entry in **Models → Engine**.
  - *What exists.* `IImageEngine` (Core/Images): load and unload a checkpoint; draw with prompt, negative prompt, size, seed, steps, sampler, scheduler, guidance, clip skip, LoRAs with strengths, hires fix, PNG/JPEG/WebP and quality, several pictures at once; progress with stage and step, optional previews, cancel. `ImageRequest.Problems()` checks a request before it reaches the engine, including that a LoRA path cannot leave the LoRA folder. `ImageServer` (Engine/Images) runs the program like the document search model: started on demand, 127.0.0.1 on a random port, `EngineEnvironment.Clean`, ends with DOG.ai, stopped after 5 idle minutes (a placeholder for step 3), plain-words errors, `Changed` and `Output` events, `IsBusy` for the idle lock later. `SdApi` speaks the server's own API over the loopback-only client. `SdServerLocator` looks only at `DOGAI_SD_SERVER` (tests) and DOG.ai's own copy: no PATH, no winget.
  - *Where it lives:* `data\engine\images` (`AppPaths.ImageEngineDirectory`; the same under a portable install's `data` folder). Not loose in `engine`, because both engines ship DLLs with the same names in different versions. Updating llama.cpp swaps the whole engine folder, so it now carries the `images` subfolder across (and stops the picture engine first, since Windows locks a running program's folder). Updating the picture engine touches only its own folder and does **not** unload the chat model. Tests install each with the other present.
  - *Download and update:* **Models → Engine** now has a **Picture engine** box (the program, or "Not installed") and, after **Check for updates**, a second row under llama.cpp: "stable-diffusion.cpp master-1094-3bad892 (CUDA 12) · Draws pictures · 0.9 GB download". It takes the two files from the newest GitHub release (the CUDA 12 build, 370 MB, and NVIDIA's runtime, 563 MB), checks each against the SHA-256 the release publishes, and refuses a file that has none. Without an NVIDIA driver, or with one older than CUDA 12, it says so instead of offering a download. A release whose files are not up yet (they arrive minutes after a release, and releases come several times a day) gets a plain "check again later".
  - *OriginPolicy: no change was needed.* The allow-list works by host, not by path, and already allows `github.com`, `api.github.com` and `*.githubusercontent.com` (where release downloads are redirected). A test pins the four addresses the picture engine uses. No host was added.
  - *Settled (the plan's open points).* (1) **Loading through the API** (`POST /sdcpp/v1/models/load`) needs a "recipe" (`sdxl-checkpoint` for this family) and file ids from the server's own folder layout (`<model root>\checkpoints`, `loras`, `vae`). It cannot load a file from any other path, and v-prediction and a separate VAE are start options anyway. So DOG.ai loads a checkpoint by starting the program with it (`-m`), which takes about half a second; the file itself is read on the first picture, as in the trial. **Unload** asks the API to let go of the memory, then ends the program. Step 4's planned folders (`<models>\image\checkpoints`, `\loras`, `\vae`) match the server's layout, so API loading stays possible later. (2) **The built-in web page cannot be switched off**, but `--serve-html-path` replaces it: DOG.ai serves a blank page there (seen in the real engine). The random loopback port remains the main protection; the API has no password option. (3) Finished pictures stay in the engine's memory 60 seconds instead of 10 minutes; pictures carry no prompt inside them (`--disable-image-metadata`, and `embed_image_metadata: false` in every request); never verbose logging. (4) The engine's output lines are **not** written to DOG.ai's log yet (step 5 adds the filter); only start-up failures are logged, and those quote only lines printed before any prompt was sent. (5) Progress stages seen in the real engine: preparing, conditioning, sampling, vae_decode, encoding (the documentation says "decoding").
  - *Checked:* 647 tests pass (40 new: the request checks; the request body; a pretend sd-server that walks a job through its states for progress, previews, failures, refusals and cancel; the start arguments; the locator ignoring PATH; the updater picking both files; damaged and checksum-less downloads; the CUDA 12 rule; the allow-list; llama.cpp and picture engine updates each keeping the other). **Real engine** (the trial's copy of the same build, and the owner's Illustrious checkpoint and three LoRAs read where they are; graphics memory was free, Ollama running with no model loaded): started in 0.5 s; a Draft picture (832×1216, 20 steps, Euler a) in 7.4 s including reading the model, with previews; the same seed gave a byte-for-byte identical picture; Cancel came back about a second after the start (three steps in); a LoRA with WebP output (141 KB); hires fix 768×768 → 1152×1152; Unload ended the program and graphics memory went back to 0.8 GB; a marker word in the prompt appeared in none of the engine's 214 output lines; the engine wrote no file beside itself or in the data folder (only DOG.ai's blank page). That test runs only when `DOGAI_SD_SERVER` and `DOGAI_TEST_IMAGE_MODEL` are set (`DOGAI_TEST_IMAGE_LORAS` optional). **In the app** (throwaway data folder): Models → Engine offline (Workbench dark) and after Check for updates with both rows showing Install, in all three looks, dark and light; no warnings in the app log.
  - *Real install (owner approved the download):* **Install** clicked in the app on a throwaway data folder: progress bar, then "stable-diffusion.cpp master-1094-3bad892 is installed", the row shows **Up to date** and the Picture engine box the program and version. Both files passed their checksums; download and unpacking took about 15 seconds. The result in `engine\images` is the same file list as the trial's hand-checked copy (the main program and DLLs compared byte for byte), plus DOG.ai's version note; 1.2 GB on disk; no download or staging folders left behind; no warnings in the app log. The real-engine test then ran against this installed copy and passed the same way (started in about 1 s, Draft picture 7.4 s, cancel about 1 s, LoRA with WebP, unload back to 0.8 GB of graphics memory, no prompt word in the output, no new files beside the engine).
  - *Not tried:* v-prediction and a separate VAE (no such files here); loading through the API; the idle stop after 5 minutes; the engine dying in the middle of a picture; a PC without an NVIDIA card (the message is unit-tested).
- **Locked data, part 2 (M8 finished, 2026-11-13): the screens, locking, and the leak checks.** The owner can now turn the lock on, live with it, and turn it off, all from inside DOG.ai.
  - *Settings → Privacy.* **Lock DOG.ai with a password…** asks for a password (at least 8 characters, typed twice), shows the 24-character recovery key with **Print…** (prints only the key and the date, through Windows' print preview) and a tick box "I have written down or printed my recovery key", then asks for two of its six groups, picked at random. Only then is `lock.json` written and DOG.ai restarted; the lock screen asks for the new password and encrypts (as built in part 1). With the lock on, the section has **Lock now** (and Ctrl+L), the idle time (Never, 5, 15, 30 minutes or 1 hour; 15 by default), **Unlock with Windows Hello on this PC** (on and off), **Change password…** (current password, new one twice; instant), **New recovery key…** (current password, then the same show-and-check steps; the old key works until the new one is confirmed), and **Turn the lock off…** (password, restart, decrypted at the lock screen). The off state and the setup window say in one sentence that losing both the password and the recovery key means the data is gone for good; the lock screen's recovery page says the same.
  - *Lock screen in the app's looks.* The lock screen (still a plain WPF window, since nothing else may run before the unlock) now wears the skin and dark or light chosen in Settings: colours, fonts, corners and buttons copied from `app.css` into `LockPalette` (keep the two in step), Fireside's serif headings, pill buttons in Fireside and Playhouse, the DOG.ai wordmark. "Match Windows" follows Windows. The look and whether the tray icon is on are copied into `lock.json` whenever they change and after every unlock.
  - *Windows Hello is offered after unlocking* (by password or recovery key) on a PC where Hello is set up but not yet used for DOG.ai: **Set up Windows Hello** or **Not now**. "Not now" is remembered per PC in `lock.json` (not carried into backups), and Settings can turn Hello on later. The part-1 tick box is gone. The Hello prompt is now brought in front of DOG.ai (Windows opens it behind desktop programs; DOG.ai looks for the prompt window and brings it forward, as Chromium and KeePassXC do). The owner confirmed every prompt came up in front.
  - *The lock screen can back out of turning the lock off:* **Keep my data locked** clears the request without needing the password.
  - *Locking:* **Lock now** in Settings, **Lock now** in the tray menu (only while the lock is on), and Ctrl+L anywhere in DOG.ai (caught by the page and by the window) restart it into the lock screen (`AppLock`, App). The old process always exits: restarting now marks DOG.ai as quitting, so close-to-tray cannot keep the old window (and its decrypted data) alive in the tray. The model and the document search engine go with the old process (seen: one llama-server running with the 27B model loaded before Ctrl+L, none while the lock screen was up); the default model loads again after unlocking, as before.
  - *Idle lock* counts from the last key press or mouse move anywhere on the PC (like a screen saver), so the lock screen appears only when nobody is there, and never earlier than the chosen time after the unlock. It waits while a reply is written, read aloud or listened for, or a model downloads, but at most one more idle period. A download cut by the lock is kept and paused; Resume in Models carries on from where it stopped. When DOG.ai was hidden in the tray, the lock screen waits in the tray too.
  - *While locked:* with the tray icon on, it shows with **Quit** only (clicking it shows the lock screen); no Ctrl+Alt+Space, no model.
  - *Restoring a locked backup* now asks for the backup's password (or recovery key) in a box under the restore buttons; a wrong one is said plainly.
  - *Leaks checked and closed.* (1) **Marker-word search** after a locked session with a real chat (Qwen3.8 27B), reading the reply aloud, dictation from a spoken test recording, a document added, indexed and searched, chat search, two exports and Lock now: no file in the data folder (192 files: database, logs, WebView2 folder) and no file in the system temp folder (2,526 files) held any of the words (the marker, the spoken words, words from the reply and the document, the password), as UTF-8 or UTF-16, any case. The control: the two exported files, searched the same way, were found. (2) **Logs** hold start, lock, unlock and engine state lines and the model file's path; nothing typed, said or read. (3) **llama-server** is started with no option that saves prompts or slots, and both engines now drop any LLAMA_ARG_ and LLAMA_LOG_ settings inherited from the environment (`EngineEnvironment`), since LLAMA_ARG_SLOT_SAVE_PATH or LLAMA_ARG_LOG_FILE would make it write text to disk; none are set on this PC today. (4) **SQLite's own temporary files** stay in memory: the cipher build is compiled with TEMP_STORE=2. (5) **Exports:** the first time a chat, card or story is exported while locked, a note says the file is a plain file anyone can read (once ever). Backups get no note: they are encrypted. (6) **WebView2 folder:** nothing found (part of the data-folder search), so it is not cleared on lock.
  - *Checked:* 607 tests pass (20 new: turning on writes nothing until confirmed; the four Settings changes and their refusals; changes keep what the lock screen added meanwhile; Hello on and off per PC and "Not now" per PC; the look saved only when changed; a part-1 lock file still reads; password rules; the recovery check; idle timing; the new settings' equality; dropped engine settings). In the app on throwaway data folders: turning on from Settings (a short password and wrong groups refused); unlocking by password, by recovery key (and the old recovery key refused after a new one), and by Windows Hello (the owner at the PC: Hello set up from the offer after a restore, used at start and after Ctrl+L, turned off and on in Settings; every Hello prompt came up in front); Lock now from Settings, the tray and Ctrl+L, each time with the old process gone; Ctrl+L pressed on the real keyboard by the owner in the published build (the lockdown that publish adds did not stop it); idle lock with the time shortened to 20 seconds, with the window open and with DOG.ai hidden in the tray, and clicking the tray icon then brought up the waiting lock screen; the tray menu "Open DOG.ai, Lock now, Quit" unlocked and "Quit" only while locked; Ctrl+Alt+Space heard while unlocked and not while locked (a log line is written when it fires, from the dictation queue, not inside the key hook); change password; new recovery key; Keep my data locked; turning off (plain database, lock file gone); a locked backup restored into a fresh DOG.ai with its password (a wrong one refused), with the chat there after unlocking; a published portable copy locked, then its folder moved: it unlocked and updated its saved paths; Print… in the published build opened Windows' print preview showing only the key and the date.
  - *Looked at in all three looks, dark and light:* the lock screen (password, with a wrong-password message), its recovery page, the "turn the lock off" lock screen, the three setup steps (password, key with Print…, the two-group check), the Privacy section with the lock off and on, the Change password, New recovery key and Turn the lock off windows, and the restore password box. Seen in fewer looks: "Enter your new password" (Workbench dark, Playhouse light), the Hello offer (Playhouse light), the TurningOn and TurningOff notices in Settings (not seen; they only show if the restart does not happen).
  - *Not tried:* idle lock waiting for a reply, reading aloud or a download (unit-tested only); the export note from the card and story exports (same code as the chat export, which was seen); what the page does after a real print or cancel (the walkthrough could not press the preview's Cancel; the page is put back when Windows reports the print finished); a PC without Windows Hello; a large database.
  - *Not explained:* one restore run went wrong in a way the logs did not show: a DOG.ai from that run outlived the script's clean-up, and the restore it drove was not found in any data folder searched (the owner's real data folder was checked and untouched). Repeated on its own debugging port, the same restore worked. The walkthrough's "wait until the lock screen closes" step had been ending early when the Hello offer replaced the password box, which may be part of it; it now waits for the whole screen. After all runs no test DOG.ai or llama-server was left running.
  - *Left as it is:* setting up Hello still asks three times (make the key, sign, sign again to confirm). The Hello key ("DOG.ai data lock") made in the owner's Windows account during the test was left there: the owner's own DOG.ai will use the same key. Clipboard and the Windows traces listed under Portable mode are not handled, as planned.
  - *Found while testing, fixed:* right after an unlock with no key press (here, the test script; for the owner, Windows Hello by face) the PC could already count as idle, and DOG.ai locked again at once. Idle time now starts at the unlock.
- **Locked data, part 1 (M8, 2026-11-13): the machinery, with a plain lock screen.** DOG.ai can now keep its database encrypted under a key that only the owner's password, recovery key or Windows Hello can produce. There is no Settings screen for it yet (part 2), so the owner cannot turn it on from the app today; everything underneath is built, tested and was tried in the real app on a throwaway data folder.
  - *What exists.* `lock.json` beside the database holds the database key wrapped three ways (password, recovery key, one Windows Hello entry per PC); a 24-character recovery key; the wait after five wrong tries (30 s, 1 min, then 5 min; it survives a restart; nothing is ever wiped); encrypting and decrypting the database in place; backups of locked data that stay locked; restoring in all four combinations of plain or locked backup into plain or locked DOG.ai; the start-up order (pending restore, portable-move check, unlock, then the database); a plain Windows lock screen with password, **Use Windows Hello**, **I forgot my password** (recovery key, then a new password) and, on first use on a PC, a tick box to turn Hello on.
  - *Turning it on is finished at the lock screen.* When a password has been chosen, only `lock.json` is written; the database is encrypted the first time that password is entered at the lock screen after the restart. So data is never encrypted under a password the owner cannot repeat, and that screen offers **Don't lock my data after all** while the data is still plain. Turning it off works the same way: asked for in the running app, carried out at the next unlock. Encrypting only ever happens at start, the one moment nothing else has the database open.
  - *Library decisions (owner, 2026-11-13).* The first library check failed: with EF Core 9's own Microsoft.Data.Sqlite (9.0.23) an encrypted database could be created through the connection string's `Password` but never reopened (it runs a query before setting the key, and current SQLite refuses any query on a file it cannot read yet). The owner chose to raise **Microsoft.Data.Sqlite to 10.0.15** (EF Core stays 9.0.23), where `Password` works. The cipher package is **`SQLite3MC.PCLRaw.bundle` 2.6.1** from the cipher's own author (SQLite 3.54.0); the older `SQLitePCLRaw.bundle_e_sqlite3mc` 2.1.11 the plan named has had no release since and carries SQLite 3.49.1, older than what DOG.ai already used (3.53.3). The new package needs SQLitePCLRaw 3.0.2 where EF Core 9 was built against 2.1.13; every test passes on that combination. `dotnet ef` still works.
  - *Settled in the checks.* Encrypting an existing file is a copy under a new key (`VACUUM INTO`), verified (integrity check, same tables, indexes and triggers, same row count in every table) and only then swapped in; `PRAGMA rekey` is not used because it refuses DOG.ai's kind of database (WAL mode). The 32-byte key is given to SQLite as a raw key, so opening a connection costs under a millisecond instead of about 20 ms. Windows Hello: on this PC a Hello key signed the same challenge to the same 256 bytes three times, also after reopening the key by name, and a different challenge gave different bytes; so the plan's first design is used and the weaker fallback is not needed.
  - *The four restore cases.* Plain backup into plain DOG.ai: as before. Plain backup into locked DOG.ai: encrypted under this DOG.ai's key while it is staged, so nothing readable waits for the restart. Locked backup into plain DOG.ai: asks for the backup's password (or its recovery key); DOG.ai then takes over the backup's lock and is locked from the next start. Locked backup into locked DOG.ai: asks for the backup's password, then re-encrypts under this DOG.ai's key, so the current password and Windows Hello keep working (decided here; the plan had not said which password should win). The database a restore replaces is kept as before; when it is encrypted, its lock file is kept beside it.
  - *A locked backup gives nothing away.* The zip holds the encrypted database, `lock.json` without Windows Hello entries, and a description that says only that it is locked, the app version and the date (not how many chats). Label "DOG AI" and entry name `dogai.db` unchanged. Plain backups are written exactly as before and older versions can still restore them.
  - *Fixed in passing.* A restored database used to run in SQLite's older journal mode from then on (a backup copy comes out that way); it is now switched back to WAL after a restore, and after encrypting or decrypting.
  - *The app now targets `net9.0-windows10.0.19041.0`* (still .NET 9; the Windows 10 part is what gives access to Windows Hello). The Debug output folder and the `D-DOGai` publish profile follow.
  - *Checked:* 587 tests pass (74 new; the 513 from before pass unchanged, also on the new SQLite packages before any other change). The new tests cover: wrap and unwrap by password, recovery key and a fake Hello; wrong password and wrong recovery key refused; password change keeps the database key; old recovery key refused after a new one; `lock.json` round-trip, damaged files refused, entries cannot be swapped; the wait times and the lock screen's logic; a current and an M7 database encrypted and decrypted back with every row intact (the M7 one migrated while encrypted); no marker word in the bytes of the encrypted file, its log file, a staged restore or a locked backup zip (packed or unpacked), with a plain backup as the control that the search finds things; search on an encrypted database; a database in use left untouched; a conversion cut short; the four restore cases and tampered locked backups; a moved portable folder's paths in an encrypted database.
  - *Tried live* (Debug build, throwaway data folder with one seeded chat; the Settings screens were stood in for by a script that writes `lock.json`): turning the lock on at the lock screen (wrong password refused, right one encrypted the database and the app opened with the chat); a locked start with wrong then right password; search in the app on the encrypted database; forgot-password with a mistyped recovery key, mismatched new passwords, then the real key typed in small letters with spaces; the old password refused afterwards; five wrong tries bringing the 30-second wait, still running after a restart, and the right password working once it was over; turning the lock off (database plain again, lock file gone, next start with no lock screen); backing out of turning it on; Windows Hello turned on with the tick box and then used to unlock with no password (the owner answered the prompts). After the locked sessions no file in the data folder (190 files: database, logs, WebView2 folder) held the chat's marker word, the password, the database key or the recovery key. A publish to a scratch folder built without warnings and ships `sqlite3mc.dll`; that portable copy was locked at its lock screen, then the whole folder was renamed and started from the new place: it unlocked, the chat was there, and the log says the saved paths were updated after the unlock. The Windows Hello key the walkthrough made in the owner's Windows account was deleted afterwards.
  - *Size:* the Windows 10 target adds `Microsoft.Windows.SDK.NET.dll` (56 MB) to a publish; the scratch publish's `app\` folder is 329 MB.
  - *Not tried:* the lock screen in light mode (only dark was seen); a locked restore in the app (the restore screen has no box for a backup's password yet, so a locked backup is refused there with a message; the logic is unit-tested); a large database (the test ones are small, so "seconds to a minute" is not measured); Windows Hello on a second PC or with face or fingerprint rather than whatever the owner used; a PC without Windows Hello (the tick box and button are simply not shown; unit-tested with a fake).
  - *Known rough edges for part 2:* turning Hello on asks for the face, fingerprint or PIN three times (make the key, sign, sign again to confirm the signature repeats); the Hello prompt can open behind the lock window; the lock screen is a plain Windows window that follows dark or light only, not the three looks (`lock.json` already has fields for skin and theme; nothing writes them yet); the recovery key is not shown anywhere in the app yet.
  - *Left for part 2:* everything under "Next" above, plus from the plan's list: tray and Ctrl+Alt+Space dead while locked seen in the app (true by construction: neither exists until after the unlock), the marker-word search after a session with a document, dictation and reading aloud, the logs and llama-server checks, and the note in the save dialog for exports.
- **Character cards in stories (2026-11-12):** a story can now have cards from Characters in it. The story setup page has a **Character cards** list (add, remove, and one tick box per card); a character's page has **Start a story**, which opens a new story with that card already in it, the card's scenario as the premise and, for an adventure, the card's first message as the opening passage (a tick box, on by default).
  - *Linked, not copied.* The story keeps only which cards are in it. Each passage is written from the cards as they are at that moment, so editing a card changes the story from the next passage on. The card's lore fires in the story as it does in a chat, and shows under **Prompt** like any other lore. Deleting a character takes it out of its stories; the stories stay.
  - *Who plays a card.* By default the narrator plays it. Tick **I play this character** (adventures) and it becomes the main character: the model is told it must never decide what that character says or does. In a written story the tick means **Main character**. Only one card can be ticked.
  - *One card or several.* A single card is sent whole (description, personality, example dialogue). With two or more, each description is cut to about 400 words and each set of examples to about 200, so the story keeps its room. The story's own Cast box stays for people who have no card.
  - *What is not taken from a card:* its own chat instructions (system prompt and post-history instruction), which are written for role-play chats, and its creator notes.
  - *The model's draft* ("Draft the story bible") is told which cards are in the story and builds around them; with a card in the story the idea box may be left empty.
  - *Choices step given more room* (800 → 2,400 tokens). Found while testing: the 27B model thinks before answering, and with a card to weigh it used all 800 tokens thinking and gave no choices. The answer itself is still a few hundred tokens.
  - Database: one additive migration (`StoryCharacters`: a list column on stories, empty for existing ones). A test upgrades an M7 database.
  - *Tried live:* with the Qwen3.8 27B model on a throwaway data folder: a story started from a card's page, opened with the card's greeting, the narrator wrote the card's character in her own voice, her lore fired when its keyword came up, choices were offered and picking one carried the story on. The setup page with one and two cards was looked at in all three looks, dark and light; the story's side panel in Workbench dark, Fireside dark and Playhouse light.
  - *Not tried:* a written (authored) story with cards against a real model (unit-tested only); the model's bible draft around a card against a real model (unit-tested only); a story with three or more long cards; cards with pictures (the test cards had none, so only the letter avatars were seen).
  - *Left out at first, done on 2026-11-13 (next entry):* a card character's lines in their own voice and colour, and turning a character chat into a story.
- **Story characters speak in their own colour and voice; a chat can become a story (2026-11-13):**
  - *Who says a line* is guessed from the names written next to it (`SpeakerGuess`, Core/Text): a tag after the line ("…," said Mara / Mara whispered), else an action just before it (Mara turned. "…"), else the speaker of the line before in the same paragraph when nobody else is named in between. A first name counts when no other card shares it; names are matched as whole words with their case, never as patterns. When unsure (a "she said" tag, a name that is spoken to, nobody named) the line gets no speaker: the plain dialogue colour and the narrator's voice. A wrong colour or voice is worse than none.
  - *Colours:* in a story with cards, a line pinned to a card takes that card's speaker colour, the same colour as the card's name in the side panel (as in group chats: by where the card's id falls among the story's cards). Turned off with the speech colours switch.
  - *Voices:* a card with a voice of its own reads its lines in that voice; narration and unsure lines are the narrator's. A card voice that is no longer on this PC reads in the narrator's voice (not the default voice, and reading does not stop). With no card voice set, reading is as before. A passage being written is read a paragraph at a time when cards have voices (who says a line can depend on words after it), sentence by sentence otherwise.
  - *Colour and voice always agree:* both use one quote finder (`Quotations`) and the same guess, and a test runs passages through both.
  - *Turn into a story* (side panel of a one-to-one character chat) opens the new-story page for an adventure that carries on from the chat: the card is linked (narrator plays it), the chat's premise comes from the card, the persona the user played becomes the main character in the Cast box, and the chat's shown branch is copied (character messages become passages, the user's become the player's actions), with its memory summary. The chat itself is not changed. Other branches, the chat's author's note, extra instructions and attached documents stay with the chat. A last message the chat never answered is left off. Only adventures: a written story's user turns are an author's directions, which chat messages are not. The last copied passage has no choices; **Suggest choices** is there.
  - *Tried live* with the Qwen3.8 27B model on a throwaway data folder: a chat with a card that has a picture and a Piper voice, a real reply, **Turn into a story** with a second card added, then an action. The copied lines were coloured by speaker and read aloud with the clips switching voice exactly where the colours switched (narrator, Mara, narrator, Mara; an unsure "She slides the map…" line in the narrator's voice). Pictures showed. The new button, the setup page's notice and the story page were looked at in all three looks, dark and light. Three long cards in a written story: the saved prompt had all three, the long card cut and every card's examples.
  - *Not seen live:* reading aloud while a passage is still being written (unit-tested), and a line's colour changing when its tag arrives a moment later.
  - *Found while testing, then fixed (next entry):* the 27B model "thinks" before writing, and in stories it thought itself out of room: in an 8,192-token context a written story with three long cards thought for 6,652 tokens and wrote nothing (without cards it wrote its passage), the bible draft with one card used its whole 3,000-token allowance thinking and failed, and once an adventure passage came back with the prose inside the "thinking".
  - *Colour clash, fixed (2026-11-13):* see "Speaker colours kept apart from plain speech" below.
- **Stories ask the model not to think (2026-11-13):** story passages, choices, rewrites, summaries of a story and the story bible draft are sent with the chat template's thinking switch off (`chat_template_kwargs`: `enable_thinking` and `thinking` false; templates without the switch ignore it, so such a model thinks as before). Chats are not affected. **Settings → Reading → Let the model think in stories** turns thinking back on (`AppSettings.StoryThinking`, off by default; `StoryOptions` in Core applies it).
  - *Tried live* with the Qwen3.8 27B model, the cases that failed with thinking: the bible draft with a card filled every field in 23 s (before: failed after 67 s); the written story with three long cards wrote a 1,746-character passage in 15 s (before: nothing after about 2½ minutes); an adventure turn gave a passage and four choices in 19 s. No thinking text was saved, so the model obeyed the switch. The prose used the cards' details and kept to the requested length, and the narrator did not decide for the player.
  - *Not compared:* the same stories with thinking on for quality side by side (with thinking on they mostly did not finish). Models other than Qwen not tried.
  - *Seen in passing:* in real prose many lines have "she said" / "he panted" tags, which the speaker guess left uncoloured; in the written passage one line in seven got a character's colour. Fixed in the next entry.
- **Tidy install folder (2026-11-13):** a publish now gives `DogAI.exe` (since renamed `DOG.ai.exe`; a small launcher, about 10 MB), `portable.txt` and an `app\` folder holding every program file; `data\` and `models\` appear beside them. Before, 318 loose files sat in the same folder as the models. The app finds its home one level up when it runs from a folder named `app` that has `portable.txt` beside it (`AppPaths.Resolve`; the old layout with the marker next to the exe still works), so saved paths and `home.txt` stay valid and nothing is rewritten. A publish empties `app\` first (only a folder named `app`, never the root), so old files cannot pile up. `tools/tidy-install.ps1` converts an existing flat install once.
  - *Checked:* 492 tests pass (two new for the layout); a publish to a scratch folder gave the layout above; the app run from `app\` created `data\` and `models\` in the root and nothing inside `app\`; the launcher started the app and exited; with `app\` missing it shows a message; a republish removed a planted stale file in `app\` and kept `models\` and `data\`, and refused to empty a publish folder not named `app`.
  - *Not tried:* voice and a real model from the new layout (a copy of the real install was not used), The real install now has this layout (see the rename entry below).
- **"She said" and "he said" find their speaker (2026-11-13):** each card's pronoun is read from its own description and personality (`Pronouns.Guess`: one kind used at least twice and three times as often as the other, else unknown). A line tagged "she said" / "he panted", or right after "She turned.", goes to the one person named nearby (in its paragraph or the one before, or who spoke there) who can be that "she" or "he". It stays plain when nobody or more than one person fits, when the person is named in the tag itself ("she said to Mara"), for "they", "I" and "you", and when someone nearby has no known pronoun. Names written in the story bible's Cast box (people without a card) now count too (`StoryPrompt.People`, `CastBoxNames`): their lines stay plain, and they keep a nearby "she" from being given to the wrong card. Colours and voices still use the same guess, paragraph by paragraph through a passage.
  - *Checked on the passages the 27B model wrote:* the written story went from one coloured line in seven to six in seven, each checked by hand against the text and all correct (the one left plain follows a paragraph that names only Ben). The adventure went from three of Mara's eleven lines to all eleven. Read aloud, exactly Mara's two lines in that passage came in her voice.
  - *Still guessing:* a person the story has not named anywhere (not a card, not in the Cast box) can't be seen, so a "she" right after such a person can still go to a card character named nearby.
- **Speaker colours kept apart from plain speech (2026-11-13):** plain speech uses each look's accent colour, and some speaker colours were the same or nearly so: Workbench's amber against speaker colours 4 and 6, Fireside's against 6 (and 4 in light), Playhouse's story accent was speaker colour 5 exactly and its characters accent speaker colour 2 exactly (so in group chats your own lines looked like one member's). Those colours are swapped, per look, for spares chosen by measured colour difference (CIEDE2000): lime or cyan in dark, teal or plum in light. Now every speaker colour is at least 19 from plain speech (before: 0 to 13, where under about 10 is easily confused) and at least 17 from each other. The spares have at least 4.5:1 contrast; one existing colour does not (light pink, speaker 2, is 4.3:1 on Playhouse's light story bubble) and was left as it was. Only `app.css` changed. Looked at in the app in all three looks, dark and light, with all six colours beside a plain line, on the story and characters screens. In light themes the new teal and the old green differ clearly by measure (22) but are alike in lightness.
- **Default model loads by itself (2026-11-13):** when no model is loaded and you open a screen with the model button (General, Characters, a group chat, a story, or the card and story editors), DOG.ai loads that section's default model (set in Models › Library › Default for…). Nothing happens if a model is loaded, loading, or failed; if no default is set; if the file is gone; or if the engine is missing. Two buttons on one screen do not both start it. If the default doesn't fit, the reason shows in the model window. Because a model unloads after 30 idle minutes, opening a chat after that brings the default back. Clicking **Unload** (or **Cancel** while loading) in the model window holds it unloaded: no default loads on its own until you load a model yourself or restart DOG.ai (`ModelLauncher.UnloadByUserAsync`). Unloads from deleting the model or updating the engine don't count. `ModelLauncher.LoadDefaultIfIdleAsync`, called from `ModelPicker`. Checked in the app: after Unload and a change of screen, General stayed on "Choose a model"; loading by hand and unloading again behaved the same. Checked in the app with the 27B model: General loaded it without a click. Not checked: Characters and Story (same code path), and the refusal message.
- **Chat folders in General (2026-11-13):** the General chat list can have folders, one level deep. A chat is filed from its side panel (**This chat → Folder**: None, any folder, or **New folder…**, which makes one and files the chat in it; a name that already exists files it in that folder). Each folder is a heading with a count that folds open or shut (remembered); the **⋯** button beside it offers **Rename** and **Delete…**. Deleting a folder never deletes chats: they move back to the main list. Pinned chats stay at the top whatever folder they are in, with the folder's name under the title. Filing a chat does not count as using it, so the list order does not change. Two folders cannot share a name (ignoring capitals). Characters and Stories have no folders.
  - Database: one additive migration (`ChatFolders`: a folder table and a folder column on chats, empty for existing ones). A test upgrades a database from the story-characters version. Folders are in the database, so backups carry them.
  - A folder's count includes its pinned chats (which are shown at the top), so a folder holding only a pinned chat is never called empty.
  - *Checked:* 513 tests pass (11 new). In the app on a throwaway data folder with sample chats: the list at rest in all three looks, dark and light; in Workbench light, making a folder from the side panel, the menu, renaming (and the refused duplicate name), folding, deleting a folder (its chat went back to the list), and that names and folded state were still there after a restart. The open menu, rename with its error, a folded folder holding the open chat, the new-folder box and the delete question were captured in all six looks; five of those twelve screenshots were looked at (Workbench dark, Fireside dark and light, Playhouse dark and light) and were right.
  - *Not done:* drag and drop onto folders, folders inside folders, search results naming a chat's folder. Not seen in the app: the note for a folder whose only chats are pinned.
- **Renamed (2026-11-12):** the app's display name is now **DOG.ai** (".ai" in the accent colour in the Fireside and Playhouse headers). Only the visible name changed: window title, tray, dialogs, help text, chat export label. Project, folder and data-folder names stayed `DOG-AI` / `DogAI` at that point (see the next entry), and the backup manifest still says "DOG AI" so older backups restore. The wordmark uses each skin's own font; a bundled display font (Space Grotesk, as in the mockup) is not added. Checked in Fireside dark only. Text written before this rename said "DOG AI"; it has since been changed to "DOG.ai".
- **Names on disk follow the brand (2026-11-13), part 1 of the DOG.ai rename:** the per-user data folder is now `%LOCALAPPDATA%\DOG.ai` and the database is `DOG.ai.db` (logs `DOG.ai-<date>.log`, backups `DOG.ai backup <date>.zip`, web user-agent `DOG.ai/0.1`, suggested models folder `<drive>:\DOG.ai\Models`). Nothing is lost on upgrade: at start the old `DOG-AI` folder is moved to the new name (`AppPaths.AdoptLegacyFolder`; never merged into an existing `DOG.ai` folder, and if Windows refuses the move the old folder is used for that run), and `dogai.db` with its `-wal`/`-shm` files is renamed (`AppPaths.RenameLegacyDatabase`; until it succeeds `DatabaseFile` points at the old file, so the app can never start on an empty database). Kept on purpose so existing data still works: the `DOGAI_*` environment variables, the backup label "DOG AI" and the `dogai.db` entry inside backup zips, the Hugging Face token key, `dogai-engine.txt`. **Part 2 (same day):** both programs are now `DOG.ai.exe` (`AssemblyName`; the launcher starts `app\DOG.ai.exe`), the icon is `DOG.ai.ico`, the publish profile is `D-DOGai` and installs to `D:\DOG.ai\app`, and a publish deletes a leftover old-named `DogAI.exe` launcher. `tools/tidy-install.ps1` accepts either exe or database name. Done by the owner on 2026-11-13: the live folder was renamed to `D:\DOG.ai` and republished. It now has the launcher, `portable.txt`, `app\`, `data\` and `Models\`, the old `D:\DOG-AI` is gone, and `data\home.txt` says `D:\DOG.ai`, so the app has started from the new place.
- **Portable mode (2026-11-12):** the published folder (`publish\win-x64`) now carries a `portable.txt`. While it is there, DOG.ai keeps everything in its own folder: `data\` (database, settings, logs, WebView2 cache, the model engine, scratch files) and `models\` (models and voice models, always this folder). The app's TEMP is pointed at `data\temp`, so llama-server and the voice libraries write their scratch files there too. A portable copy uses only its own engine (Models › Engine), never winget's. Moving or copying the folder is allowed: on the next start, saved model paths (settings, default models, last model, assistants, download records) are moved to the new place. Debug builds have no marker and keep using `%LOCALAPPDATA%\DOG.ai`. Delete `portable.txt` to go back to that.
  - *Tried live:* a copy of the publish in a temporary folder whose name has a space. Settings › About showed "Portable" and the folder's own data and models paths. With no engine in the folder, the model picker said to get it in Models › Engine. With the winget engine named by `DOGAI_LLAMA_SERVER` and `D:\DOG-AI\Models` as the models folder, the 27B model loaded on the graphics card and answered. Afterwards `%LOCALAPPDATA%\DOG.ai` was unchanged, and a scan of LocalAppData, Roaming, Documents and TEMP found nothing from DOG.ai. Only other programs' files had changed: the Claude app, Edge, Windows sign-in caches, a Windows jump list and the NVIDIA driver's shader cache (`NVIDIA\DXCache`, written by the graphics driver for any program that draws on the GPU). One short-lived `.tmp` appeared in the system TEMP during the run and was gone before it could be looked at; the test scripts' PowerShell is the likely writer, but this is not proven.
  - *Not tried:* installing the engine through Models › Engine into a portable folder (that download goes online); actually moving a used portable folder in the app (the path rewrite is unit-tested); voice in portable mode; only Workbench dark was looked at (the change is one row in About and one message, both using existing components).
  - *Outside the app's control, said plainly:* Windows still keeps its own traces of any program it runs (prefetch, recent-file lists, file-dialog history, the tray icon's history, crash reports). WebView2 itself is the shared copy Windows 11 installs. The Hugging Face token is encrypted for this Windows user, so on another PC or account it must be typed again.
- **What M7 delivered (Polish):**
  - *Search* (rail icon in Workbench, magnifier at the top right in Fireside and Playhouse): type words, every word must appear, the last one may be unfinished. It searches every message of every General chat, character chat, group chat and story, and chat names (those come first), with the matched words marked. At most three hits per chat. Opening a hit shows the branch that holds the message (even one you had switched away from), scrolls to it and flashes it.
  - *Assistants and prompts* (General → "Assistants and prompts"): an **assistant** is instructions + a style + optionally a model; a chat's side panel has an **Assistant** list that copies the instructions and style into the chat. If the assistant names a model that is not loaded, a note offers **Load it** / **Swap to it**; nothing is swapped silently. A **prompt** is saved wording with blanks, `[[name]]` or `[[name|default]]`; the book button beside the message box (every mode) lists them, asks for each blank, and puts the finished text in the box. Three example assistants (Coder, Editor, Tutor) and three prompts (Summarise, Translate, Review code) come with a new install and can be edited or deleted.
  - *Backup and restore* (Settings): **Back up now…** writes one zip with everything you made; **Restore from a backup…** checks the file, then **Restart and restore** closes DOG.ai, swaps the data in and starts it again.
  - *Pin chats* (General): pinned chats stay at the top of the list. *Save as markdown* (General): the branch on screen as a .md file.
  - *Code colours and maths in replies:* code blocks are coloured (highlight.js, about 40 common languages) and `$…$` / `$$…$$` formulas are drawn (KaTeX). Both are bundled files; nothing is fetched.
  - *Links ask first:* a web link in a reply or a model card opens a prompt showing the real address; **Open in my browser** hands it to Windows. Before, links did nothing. Only http, https and mailto can be opened.
  - *Windows extras, all off until ticked (Settings → Windows):* a notification-area icon (click to open, right-click Quit), closing the window keeps DOG.ai running there, and **hold Ctrl+Alt+Space in any program to talk; what you said is typed where your cursor is when you let go**.
  - *Self-contained publish:* `dotnet publish src/DogAI.App -p:PublishProfile=win-x64` gives `publish\win-x64` (274 MB), which runs on a PC with nothing installed. It keeps only Windows 64-bit native files (Whisper's Linux, macOS, ARM and 32-bit libraries and the .pdb files are dropped).
  - Database: one additive migration (`Polish`: a pinned flag on chats, and the full-text index with its three triggers, filled from existing messages). A test upgrades an M6 database.
- **How M7 behaves, decided while building it:**
  - *Search uses SQLite's FTS5* (checked at test time that the bundled SQLite has it). The index holds no second copy of the text; triggers keep it in step on insert, edit and delete, including when a whole chat is deleted. Typed words are quoted one by one, so nothing typed can act as query syntax. Snippets are drawn as text with `<mark>` around matches, never as HTML.
  - *Search finds the markdown source of a reply*, so a link shows as `[text](address)` in a hit. Words from the model's hidden "thinking" are not searched.
  - *Assistants copy, they do not link.* Editing an assistant later changes nothing in chats already started from it. The side panel stops showing an assistant's name once the chat's instructions no longer match it.
  - *Blanks use double square brackets* because `{{char}}` and `{{user}}` already mean something in character cards, and single braces and brackets are everywhere in code.
  - *A backup is the database, copied consistently (`VACUUM INTO`) while the app runs, plus a small description file.* Everything the user makes lives in that one file (character pictures, document text and vectors, voices, styles, assistants, prompts, settings). It does **not** hold models, voice models or the engine. The Hugging Face token is removed from the backup.
  - *A restore never touches the database while the app runs* (background indexing writes to it and connections are pooled). The file is checked first (a real DOG.ai zip with only the two expected entries and no paths that climb out, a healthy SQLite file, not made by a newer version) and parked in a `restore` folder; the next start swaps it in before anything opens the database, and normal migrations bring an older backup up to date. The replaced database is kept in `before-restore`. This PC's own models folder, extra model folders, download token, microphone and speakers are kept, so restoring on another PC does not point at folders it lacks; a models folder on a drive this PC does not have is replaced by the usual suggestion.
  - *Pinned does not count as using a chat*, so pinning does not reorder the recent chats.
  - *Code is coloured after it is drawn.* The code block goes into the page as one piece of markup, HTML-encoded, and `enhance.js` colours what is inside it; that way Blazor never loses track of its own nodes. Formulas are the same: the page only marks them, KaTeX draws them, and a `$5 … $10` price is not taken for a formula.
  - *The link prompt must sit inside the themed wrapper* (`MainLayout`'s `.shell`), or its dialog loses all colours.
  - *A restore that is waiting stays visible:* Settings shows it with **Restart and restore** and **Cancel restore** whenever one is queued, even after leaving and coming back. Before the old database is moved aside, its write-ahead log is folded in (and anything left of it is kept with the safety copy), so the safety copy holds the newest writes even after a crash.
  - *The global key needs a low-level keyboard hook* (on a thread of its own with its own message loop, because Windows removes a slow hook without telling anyone) because Windows' simpler hot key call never reports a key being let go. The hook looks only at Space together with Ctrl and Alt, swallows that combination so the other program does not see it, and does nothing while DOG.ai's own window has focus (the message box has its own Ctrl+Space). Typed text is sent as Unicode characters, so it can never set the hook off again; typing waits (up to 2 s) until Ctrl and Alt are up so no shortcut fires in the other program. A program running with more rights than DOG.ai (an administrator window) does not accept typed text.
  - *Publish:* each release folder is built for win-x64 with ReadyToRun code. WebView2 is not included (Windows 11 has it).
- **Tried live** with a throwaway data folder and chats written straight into it (no model loaded): search in all three looks, dark and light (word match, prefix match, title match, a hit on a branch that was not on screen); the assistants and prompts page, the prompt picker with its blanks and defaults, and applying an assistant to a chat, in all looks; the link prompt in all looks; Settings → Windows and Backup in all looks; a colour-coded code block and a formula in a reply; a `<script>` tag in a reply stayed text; pinning and the pinned order; **backup through the real Save dialog, deleting a chat, restore through the real Open dialog, "Restart and restore" (DOG.ai closed and started itself again, swapped the data in, the deleted chat was back, search still worked)**; the published build started from `publish\win-x64` and, with the recorded-question stand-in for the microphone, Whisper on the graphics card wrote the question down and Kokoro spoke a reply (so the native voice libraries load from the trimmed layout); the tray and key settings were turned on and DOG.ai started with no hook error logged.
- **Ctrl+Alt+Space in another program:** tried by the owner with a real microphone and it works (2026-11-13): what was said was typed into the other program.
- **Tray icon:** tried by the owner and it works (2026-11-13).
- **Not tried, said plainly:** a restore on a different PC or from an older real backup (an M6-shaped database upgrade is tested, and restoring a backup into a folder with different settings is unit-tested), a backup of a large library (hundreds of MB of documents; speed unmeasured), **Save as markdown** and **Pin to top** through the real UI beyond seeing the pinned chat on top, an assistant whose model file is set (the note and load button were not shown), searching a story and a character chat in the app (unit-tested through the database), and running the published build on a PC without .NET. Story passages and message hits in story player turns do not scroll to the message (the chat still opens on the right branch). The prompt (book) button also appears in the Characters, group and Story message boxes; it was looked at in General only.
- **Left out of M7, still owed:**
  - ~~*Chat folders* (General list).~~ Done on 2026-11-13 (see "Chat folders in General" above).
  - *Titles written by the model.* Chats are still named from their first line.
  - *Editing the global key* (it is fixed at Ctrl+Alt+Space), *barge-in*, *Kokoro on the graphics card* and the other M6 items below.
  - *Search over the text of documents* (Documents has its own "Try a search"), and search results that jump to the exact passage of a story.
  - *An installer.* The publish gives a folder; zip it or wrap it in an installer later. The app has no auto-update (nothing may go online outside Models).
  - *Highlight.js knows about 40 common languages* only; others stay plain.
- **What M6 delivered (Voice):**
  - *Talking instead of typing:* a microphone button under every message box (General, Characters, groups, Story). Press it, talk, press it again, and what you said appears in the box to check before sending. Holding **Ctrl+Space** in the box does the same. A line under the box says what is happening (listening, writing it down) with a level meter.
  - *Hands-free:* the second button keeps the microphone on. Talk, pause, and it is sent; the reply is read aloud if reading is on; then it listens again. It does not listen while a reply is being written or read, so DOG.ai never hears itself. Sensitivity (noisy room, normal, quiet room) is in Settings.
  - *Reading aloud:* **Read aloud** under every reply and story passage (it becomes **Stop reading** while speaking). The speaker button under the message box reads every new reply as it is written, a sentence at a time, starting with the first sentence while the rest is still coming; a stop button replaces it while speaking. Code blocks, web addresses, citation numbers and markup symbols are not read. Drafts ("Write mine", rewrites), story choices and "thinking" are never read.
  - *Voices:* the 54 Kokoro v1.0 voices (American and British English plus eight other languages), Piper voices, and the voices that come with Windows (always there, no download). Each character has a voice in its editor, each story a narrator's voice on its setup page, both with a **Listen** button; everything else uses the default voice from Settings (if none is chosen: Kokoro's "Heart", else any downloaded voice, else a Windows voice). In a group chat each member speaks with its own voice, one after another, never over each other. A voice that is no longer on the PC falls back to the default and the picker says so.
  - *Models → Voice:* recommended speech recognition models (Whisper large-v3 turbo, small, base English) and voices (Kokoro v1.0, four Piper voices with open licences), with Download, **Use this** (which Whisper model listens) and Delete. Downloads go through the normal download list with checksums, into a "voice" folder inside the models folder. Voice models found there but not on the list are shown too.
  - *Settings → Voice:* default voice and speed, read replies aloud, speakers, microphone, the language you speak (or detect it), hands-free sensitivity, and **Try listening**.
  - Database: one additive migration (a voice column on characters and a narrator voice on stories, empty for existing ones). A test upgrades an M5 database.
- **How M6 behaves, decided while building it:**
  - *Everything runs inside DOG.ai, on this PC.* Whisper (whisper.cpp through Whisper.net) listens; Kokoro and Piper (sherpa-onnx) and Windows' own voices speak; NAudio records and plays. Recording and playing happen in the app, not the web view, so the web view still needs no microphone permission and its security policy is unchanged.
  - *Whisper runs on the graphics card (Vulkan) when it can.* Measured here with large-v3 turbo: a 7-second sentence takes 0.33 s on the RTX 4090 and 13 s on the processor, so the processor is only the fallback. The first run ever on a graphics card prepares its shaders (about 15 s, once; the driver keeps them), so the model is loaded and tried as soon as the microphone turns on. It uses about 1 GB of graphics memory and lets go after ten idle minutes.
  - *Voices run on the processor.* Kokoro says 7 seconds of speech in about 1.6 s, Piper faster; the next sentence is made while the current one plays, so there are no gaps. A voice pack is let go after ten idle minutes; at most two are kept loaded.
  - *sherpa-onnx stays on version 1.13.* Version 2 dropped the espeak-ng pronunciation engine: Piper voices could not say anything and Kokoro skipped every number. Tried and measured before choosing.
  - *Each Kokoro voice pronounces through espeak-ng with that engine's own language name*: "en-us", "en" for British (it has no "en-gb"; the first version used that and British voices said nothing, found by the owner), "cmn" for Mandarin. A voice that cannot say a text now reports it in words instead of failing.
  - *Kokoro's voice names are read from the model file itself*, so a newer pack with more voices works without a code change.
  - *Whisper's made-up text is dropped.* Given silence or noise it tends to write "Thank you." or "Thanks for watching!"; those, noise markers like "[BLANK_AUDIO]", and anything the model itself rates as probably not speech are thrown away instead of typed into the box.
  - *Hands-free finds speech by loudness against the room's background level*, which it keeps learning while nobody talks; it keeps 0.4 s from before the start so the first word is not cut, ends after a 0.9 s pause, drops clicks shorter than 0.3 s, and cuts at 30 s.
  - *Starting a new reply stops what is being read*, unless group members are answering one after another; then each waits for the one before.
  - *Devices are saved by name*, not number, so plugging something in does not change the choice. Windows' older sound interface, which DOG.ai uses, cuts names at 31 characters ("Headphones (Arctis Nova Pro Wir").
  - *Voice choices are DOG.ai's own*: a character's voice is not written into exported cards.
  - *Test stand-ins:* `DOGAI_TEST_MIC` (a WAV file played as the microphone) and `DOGAI_TEST_SPEAKERS` (a folder that receives what would have been played) let walkthroughs drive voice without hardware.
- **Tried live** with Mag-Mell 12B, Whisper large-v3 turbo, Kokoro v1.0 and Piper Amy (downloaded from Hugging Face with permission; checksums matched), using the test stand-ins for microphone and speakers: a recorded question was written down word for word with the microphone button, sent, and the reply read aloud sentence by sentence (the saved clips, run back through Whisper, said exactly the reply); hands-free heard the same question, sent it, waited while the reply was written and read, then listened again; a character given the Piper voice in its editor was read in that voice (and the Read aloud / Stop reading button worked); a character's voice and the chosen speakers were still shown after leaving and coming back; and the Kokoro pack was downloaded again through Models → Voice into a fresh folder (375 files, every one checked, no partial files left) and its voices then appeared in the pickers. An opt-in test (`DOGAI_TEST_VOICE_DIR`) has Kokoro, Piper and a Windows voice each say a sentence that Whisper must hear back; it passed. Screens checked in all three looks, dark and light: Settings → Voice, Models → Voice, the character editor's voice picker, the story setup's narrator voice, and the message box while listening.
- **Real hardware:** the owner heard playback on real speakers (voice **Listen** buttons and replies read aloud in a chat, 2026-11-12) and tried a real microphone, which worked (2026-11-13). Every other live run used the test stand-ins.
- **Group chats and stories read aloud:** tried by the owner and they work (2026-11-13).
- **Not tried, said plainly:** how Kokoro's non-English voices sound (each language was checked to produce speech, and three British voices were heard back correctly by Whisper), the four recommended Piper voices (Amy, which is not on the list because its licence is unclear, was used instead), Whisper small and base, and a language other than "detect".
- **Left out of M6, still owed:**
  - *Talking over a reply to stop it* ("barge-in"). Hands-free waits until reading has finished; there is no echo cancellation.
  - ~~*A push-to-talk key that works anywhere in Windows.*~~ Done in M7 (Ctrl+Alt+Space, opt-in; see M7 for what was and was not tried).
  - *Kokoro on the graphics card.* The sherpa-onnx package has no GPU build for Windows; it is quick enough on the processor.
  - *Story narration and "What do you do?"*: passages are cleaned after they are written, but reading starts while they stream, so a closing question the model adds may be read before it is removed.
  - Full device names (would need the newer Windows sound interface, WASAPI).
  - ~~The app folder grows by the voice libraries' native files for every platform.~~ Done in M7: the publish profile keeps only Windows x64.
- **What M5 delivered (Documents):**
  - *Reading files:* PDF (its text layer, page by page), Word .docx (headings, lists and tables kept), markdown, text and code files. The text is copied into DOG.ai, so the original can move. A scanned PDF with no text, an old .doc, a password-protected file or a binary file is refused with a plain reason. Each file is cut into passages of about 800 characters at paragraph and line boundaries, overlapping a little, each knowing its page and heading.
  - *Document search model:* a second, small llama-server runs the embedding model beside the chat model (about 1 GB of graphics memory for Qwen3 Embedding 0.6B). It starts when first needed and stops after ten idle minutes. It is the library model marked **Default for… → Document search**; without a choice, the first embedding model found. Embedding models are recognised from their header, are not offered for chat, and have no Load or Benchmark buttons. Changing the model indexes every document again in the background.
  - *Chat with documents (General):* **Add files…** in the side panel's Documents section adds files to that chat only. **Document collections** (link at the top of the chat list) holds shared sets of files; tick a collection in any chat's Documents panel to search it. The collections page lists each file's state (waiting, indexing with progress, ready, failed with a reason and **Try again**) and has **Try a search** to see what a question finds.
  - *Citations:* before each reply, the newest message (with the previous turn when it is short) is searched; the best passages go with that message, numbered, and the model is asked to cite them as [1], [2]. Cited numbers in the reply are small buttons, and a **Sources** row under the reply lists every passage given. Either opens the passage inside its document, marked, with **Open the file** and **Copy passage**. The prompt inspector lists the passages.
  - *Reference documents in character chats, groups and stories:* the same Documents panel; passages go in as background, not to be cited.
  - *Recall in long chats and stories:* when older messages are left out to fit, the left-out ones that match the newest message are found with the search model and sent along, next to the rolling summary. A switch under Memory turns it off per chat. The inspector says how many were recalled.
  - Database: one additive migration (collections, documents, passages, chat-collection links, message vectors; a sources column on messages; a recall switch on chats, on for existing ones). A test upgrades an M4 database.
- **How M5 behaves, decided while building it:**
  - *Found passages and recalled messages go with the newest user message, never in the system message.* llama-server reuses the prompt it processed last time up to the first change; text that changes every turn in the system message would mean reading the whole chat again for every reply. The standing "cite the passages" instruction stays in the system message, since it does not change.
  - *Search combines meaning and exact words.* Passages are ranked by vector similarity and by keyword scoring (BM25) and the two rankings are merged, so a code like "E4012" or a name is found even when the meaning score alone is weak.
  - *Thresholds were measured, not guessed:* with Qwen3 Embedding 0.6B a question and its answer passage scored 0.55–0.75, other passages of the same documents 0.27–0.43, unrelated text under 0.2. Passages need 0.45 (or 0.25 with a strong exact-word match); recalled messages need 0.5, because a wrong memory misleads more than a missing one.
  - *Passages are short (about 800 characters).* The first live try used 1,200 and a whole PDF page that mixed maintenance, error codes and warranty scored 0.28 against a question it answered.
  - *The passage list says which numbers exist* ("numbered 1 to 3"). Without it the model also cited the manual's own section numbers ([4], [5]); numbers that are not sources stay plain text.
  - *Vectors belong to one model.* Each is stored with the model's file name and only compared with vectors from the same model. Messages are embedded the first time recall needs them and again only if edited.
  - *"Open the file" opens documents in their usual program, but code and scripts in Notepad*, so opening a .bat or .ps1 shows it rather than running it.
  - *Problems never block a reply.* Without a search model, or if it fails to start, the reply is written without passages and says why under it.
- **Tried live** with Mag-Mell 12B (ChatML) and Qwen3 Embedding 0.6B Q8_0 (downloaded from Hugging Face with permission; checksum matched): a General chat with a 3-page PDF manual and a Word file (two answers citing the right passages, citation buttons and the Sources row opening the passage viewer, the inspector listing the passages); a shared collection with **Try a search** finding the error-code page; an interactive story with a world file as reference (the narrator got the matching sections) and, with a 4,096-token context, recall of left-out passages (in one run two passages about meeting the guild master were recalled; in a rerun with the final 0.5 threshold nothing left out matched closely enough, so none were). Screens checked in all three looks, dark and light: the General chat with files and cited replies, the passage viewer, and the collections page.
