The Documents panel and recall switch in the story and character side panels were used by the test but sit below the visible part of the panel, so they were not looked at on screen.
- **Left out of M5, still owed:** drag-and-drop of files onto the window (files are added with the Windows dialog); scanned PDFs (would need OCR); old .doc, .odt, .rtf, HTML-as-document and e-books; reranking with a second model; standalone lorebooks (still owed from M3). Not tried live: a large document (hundreds of pages; indexing speed is unmeasured), changing the search model with documents already indexed (unit-tested), a group chat with documents, and recall in a character chat (unit-tested; tried live in a story).
- **What M4 delivered (Story):**
  - *Two kinds of story*, chosen when you press **New story**: an **interactive adventure** (you are the main character; the model narrates and you choose) and a **written story** (you are the author; the model writes passage by passage along your outline).
  - *Story bible* for both: title, premise, genre, tone, point of view, tense, cast, world, style guide. It is sent with every passage and can be changed at any time (**Story bible** in the side panel); changes apply from the next passage. **Draft the story bible** has the loaded model fill it in from a one-line idea (and, for a written story, an outline of chapters), using the same structured-output method as the card builder. Nothing is saved until you press Create.
  - *Interactive adventure:* **Begin the story** writes an opening. After every passage a second, short request asks the model for two to four choices as structured output, so they always arrive well-formed; press one, or type your own action in the box. **Other choices** asks again. Optional per story: **state tracking** (location, inventory, stats, facts; the model updates them after each passage, they are sent with your next action, and you can correct them in the side panel) and **dice** (the model marks risky choices; pressing one, or ticking "Roll dice" for your own action, rolls a 20-sided dice and the narrator is told the result: 1 critical failure, 2–7 failure, 8–12 partial success, 13–19 success, 20 critical success).
  - *Branch map:* every path taken, drawn as a tree. Pick any point to go back to it; what you do next starts a new branch and nothing is lost. **Go back to here** under any earlier passage does the same. Each branch has its own state, so going back also brings back the inventory and stats from then.
  - *Written story:* **Write next** writes the next passage, optionally steered by a line you type first. A box above it says which outline chapter the passage belongs to; pick the next chapter when you are ready to move on, and a chapter heading appears. Select any text in a passage and a bar offers **Rewrite, Expand, Shorten, Change tone, Other**; the replacement is shown beside the original and nothing changes until you press **Use this**.
  - *Both kinds:* every passage can be rewritten (the earlier version is kept; arrows flip between versions), continued, edited by hand, copied, and inspected (**Prompt** shows exactly what the model was sent). Style and memory work as in chats: when the story outgrows the context, older passages are summarised.
  - *Save a copy* of the branch on screen as **Markdown**, a single **web page**, or an **EPUB** e-book (title page, contents, one file per chapter). The files contain nothing that points outside themselves.
  - The model picker in Story labels the default model for Story.
  - Database: one additive migration (a `Stories` table and a nullable `ExtraJson` column on messages). A test upgrades an M3 database.
- **How M4 behaves, decided while building it:**
  - *A story is a conversation.* Passages are replies in the same message tree as chats, so versions, branching, continue, the prompt inspector and memory needed no second engine. The player's actions (interactive) and the author's directions (written) are the user turns. Story settings live in their own table; choices, state, dice rolls and chapter numbers are kept with each message.
  - *Choices come from a second request, not from the passage itself.* The passage streams as ordinary prose (better writing, and you can read it as it arrives); then the same prompt plus the passage is sent again with a JSON schema for the choices and state. The engine reuses the prompt it has just processed, so this takes a second or two.
  - *The narrator is reminded of the rules next to your newest action* (length, do not ask what the player does, the dice result, the current state), because models follow what is nearest the end. A closing "What do you do?" that slips through anyway is removed from the saved passage, since the choices are right below it.
  - *Rewriting a selection is held to the selection's size:* the request states the length in words, the reply is capped in tokens, and the temperature is lowered. The first live try, without this, turned one sentence into a page.
  - *Rewriting a selection changes the passage in place* (after you accept it). Rewriting a whole passage keeps the old version beside it.
  - *Going back never deletes.* In a written story, writing on from an earlier passage makes the later text another version; the arrows on the direction line switch between them.
  - *A turn that got no passage* (a problem, or stopped at once) is replaced by the next one rather than followed by it, so two user turns never go to the model in a row.
  - *New stories start with the Creative style.* Dice are off and state tracking is on unless changed.
- **Tried live** with Mag-Mell 12B (ChatML template): an adventure from a one-line idea through bible draft, opening, choices, a typed action with dice, state updates, the branch map (going back and branching), other choices, editing the state by hand (it was still there after leaving and returning), continue, and hand-editing a passage; a written story through bible and six-chapter outline draft, three passages with a direction and a chapter change, rewriting a selection four ways, rewriting a passage, editing the outline afterwards, and all three exports through the real Save dialog. All story screens (landing, new story, adventure, branch map, written story with the selection bar, rewrite dialog, story bible page) were looked at in all three looks, dark and light.
- **What the live tries showed about the model, not fixed by code:** passages run about 350–450 words when asked for 250. The dice result was followed in two of three rolls after the wording was strengthened (a failed roll was once still narrated as a success). Rewrites of a selection sometimes drift into the sentence that follows. A stronger model should do better on all three.
- **Left out of M4, still owed:**
  - *Hierarchical summaries* (scene → chapter → story so far). There is one rolling "story so far" summary, the same mechanism as chat memory. It was not run live in a story (unit-tested).
  - *Lorebooks and document collections as world reference.* Standalone lorebooks are still owed from M3; documents are M5. The bible's World field is the place for world facts until then.
  - ~~*Narrator read-aloud* comes with voice in M6.~~ Done in M6.
  - *Scene beats as their own level.* The outline is chapters, each with a free-text plan; beats can be written into the plan.
  - A past action or direction cannot be edited; go back and do something else instead. The branch map is for adventures only.
  - Not tried: opening the EPUB in an e-reader (its structure and well-formedness are unit-tested), a strict-template model (Gemma, Mistral `[INST]`; role alternation is unit-tested), a reasoning model, and a story long enough to need the summary.
- **What M3 part 3 delivered (group chat):**
  - *Group chats* live in the Characters section, under "Group chats" in the list, with a **New group** button (shown once there are two characters). Pick two or more characters and a turn order, then start. A group keeps its members in its own table; deleting a character takes it out of groups but keeps the chats and what it said ("Removed character").
  - *Turn order*, changeable at any time in the side panel: **Natural** (whoever your message names answers, by full or first name, in the order named; if nobody is named, whoever has been quiet longest), **Everyone in turn** (every member who is not muted answers once, in list order; Stop ends the whole round), **You choose** (nobody answers until you pick). Under the message box a row of member buttons makes one of them speak now, and **Anyone** lets turn order pick (someone the last speaker named, else the quietest, never the one who just spoke). Members can be reordered, muted (they stay in the scene but only speak when picked), removed and added.
  - *Each speaker gets its own prompt:* its own card (and its own main instruction, if the card has one) with a group version of the default instruction ("…never write the words or actions of {{user}} or of Ann and Bo"), a short note on every other member (their description, cut to about 700 characters, each card's {{char}} filled with its own name), lore from every member's book, the chat's scene/instructions, author's note and memory.
  - *Other characters' replies are named in the history* ("Ann: …"). Replies from several characters in a row are joined into one assistant turn, and the prompt always ends with a short user turn "[Write Bo's next reply.]", so roles strictly alternate (strict templates such as Gemma and Mistral refuse anything else, and llama-server would treat a trailing assistant turn as text to continue). A leading "Bo:" the model copies is removed from the reply, and writing stops if a line starts with another member's or the user's name.
  - *Regenerate, swipe and continue keep the same speaker.* The opening message's alternatives are every member's greetings, each member's V3 `group_only_greetings` first, so the arrows choose who opens the scene and how. One-to-one chats never use group-only greetings. The card editor has a new **Group-only greetings** section; they are saved inside the card and exported.
  - *Memory and "Write mine"* work in groups: summaries label each line with its speaker; the draft is told not to speak for any member.
  - The side-panel settings shared by character and group chats (persona, style, instructions, author's note, memory) moved into one component, `RoleplayPanels`.
  - Fixed on the way: the summary request was built with `string.Format`, which turned `{{char}}` into `{char}`, so the names were never filled in (the model was asked about "the story so far between {char} and {user}"). It also said "write an summary".
  - Database: one additive migration (a `TurnOrder` column and a new `ChatMembers` table). A test upgrades a part 2 database.
- **How M3 part 3 behaves, decided while building it:**
  - *One opening slot, not one greeting per member.* Chaining a greeting from each member would hang the second greeting under one version of the first, so flipping the first would hide it. Others join once the user writes, or when picked.
  - *Muted members do not answer even when named*; pressing their button is the way to hear them.
  - *A group chat is one chat*, not a reusable "group" with several chats. **New chat** in the side panel starts a fresh chat with the same members, order, mutes and turn order.
  - *Lore budget in a group* is the largest budget any member's book sets, at most a quarter of the context.
- **Tried live** with Mag-Mell 12B (ChatML template) and three test characters: naming Pip made Pip answer; with "Everyone in turn" all three answered in list order, each in character, none writing another's lines; the copied "Name:" prefixes were removed; regenerating kept the same speaker. The prompt inspector showed the alternating prompt described above. Screens checked in all three looks, dark and light (group chat and the new-group form). **Not tried:** a model with a strict template (Gemma, Mistral `[INST]`; none on this PC, so alternation is guarded by unit tests only), the member up/down/mute/add buttons and the group-only greetings section of the card editor were seen on screen but not clicked through, and "Write mine" and memory were not run live in a group (unit-tested).
- **What M3 part 2 delivered:**
  - *Prompt in parts.* The system message is now built from parts: instruction, lore placed before the card, card and persona, lore placed after the card, example dialogue, the chat's own instructions, and the memory summary (`PromptLayout`, Core). Older turns are trimmed first. If even the newest message does not fit, example dialogue goes next, then memory, then lore; the instruction and card always stay. The card's final instruction and the author's note go *inside user turns*, never as an extra system message, because strict chat templates refuse a system message anywhere but first.
  - *Lore.* The card's embedded lorebook is used. Recent messages are searched for each entry's keywords (whole words; `/pattern/` keys are regular expressions with a 50 ms limit, since cards are untrusted). Entries can need a second keyword, be always on, match capitals exactly, or bring in other entries. The lore budget is the book's own, at most a quarter of the context. A **Lore** page (button on the character's page) edits entries; they are saved back into the card, so export gives them to other apps with unknown fields intact. **Import lore…** reads SillyTavern World Info files or another card's book. The prompt inspector lists the entries used.
  - *Author's note* per chat in the side panel, placed with the newest message or 1–8 messages back (default 4).
  - *"Write mine"* (impersonate) next to Send: the model drafts your next message into the box for you to edit. Nothing is saved.
  - *Memory.* When older messages stop fitting, they are summarised (in chunks if needed) and the summary is sent in their place. The summary runs a few messages ahead of what is trimmed so it is not rewritten after every reply. The side panel shows what it covers, lets you correct it, switch memory off, or forget it. The inspector shows when a summary was used.
  - *AI card builder.* On the new-character page, describe a character in a sentence and the loaded model fills in every field. The engine is asked for JSON matching a schema (structured output), so the answer always parses. The draft is not saved until you press Create.
  - *Avatar cropping.* After choosing a picture (characters and personas) a cropper shows the square used in lists and chats: drag it, move it with the arrow keys, size it with the slider, and see a round preview. Characters keep the full picture, so **Crop** can change it later; personas keep only the avatar, so they are cropped when the picture is chosen.
  - Database: one additive migration (new columns only). Chats from M3 part 1 get note depth 4 and memory on; a test upgrades a part 1 database.
- **How M3 part 2 behaves, decided while building it:**
  - *The summary belongs to one branch.* It records the last message it covers and is only used while that message is on the branch shown. Switching to another greeting or an earlier edit means a new summary is written when needed, replacing the old one (one summary per chat). Editing a reply's text in place does not mark the summary out of date.
  - *Bad summaries are refused.* Small models sometimes repeat the request back, loop ("User 2: User 2: …"), or run to the length limit. Such text is thrown away rather than sent with every later reply, and the next try waits a few messages. The request sits after the messages, where small models follow it best, with repetition penalties on.
  - *Impersonate writes inside an open user turn* (raw completion from where the user's turn starts in the model's own template), which makes the model speak as the user much more reliably than asking it to. If the chat already ends with an unanswered message of yours, it asks instead. The text the template puts after a user's message is used as a stop string (and the draft is cut there too), so on templates whose turn end is ordinary text, like Mistral's `[/INST]`, the draft cannot run on into the character's reply; that case is unit-tested only.
  - *Lore matches whole words* so "cat" does not fire on "category". A keyword containing a comma cannot be typed in the editor (commas separate keywords); imported ones are kept.
  - *Lore is searched in the last 4 messages* unless the book says otherwise (SillyTavern's default is 2).
- **Tried live** with two local role-play models: Self After Dark 8B (Llama 3 template) and Mag-Mell 12B (ChatML template). Lore fired and was listed, the note and final instruction landed in user turns, the card builder produced a full card, cropping saved the chosen square, and memory worked in a 4,096-token context (after a fix: the first run left 35 messages out but summarised only 31, because the summary's own size pushed out more turns; now the check is repeated with the summary in place, and the final run covered 41 with 37 left out). The 12B model wrote good summaries and good "Write mine" drafts. The 8B model did not: it echoed the request back or looped when summarising, and in a fresh chat its draft was a placeholder. That is what the refusal checks above guard against. **Not tried:** a model with a strict template (Mistral `[INST]`, Gemma), a reasoning model summarising, and a SillyTavern World Info file from the wild (the import is tested with a sample).
- **Left out of M3 part 2, still owed:** standalone lorebooks shared between characters or attached to a chat (lore lives in each card for now; World Info import copies entries into a card); the SillyTavern "character's note" (`extensions.depth_prompt`) is kept on export but not used.
- **What M3 part 1 delivered:**
  - *Characters section.* Import Character Card V1/V2/V3 from PNG or JSON; export as a PNG card (V2 and V3 inside, as other apps expect) or as V3 JSON. Fields DOG.ai does not edit (extensions, the embedded lorebook, V3-only fields) are kept and written back unchanged.
  - *Card editor* with every standard field, extra greetings, a picture, a style for new chats, and a live token count (exact when a model is loaded).
  - *Personas:* who you are in a chat (name, description, picture). One is the default; each chat can switch.
  - *Character chat:* greetings are alternatives you flip through with the arrows; the last reply always shows arrows, and the right arrow past the newest writes another version (swipe). The prompt inspector shows the full card text sent.
  - *Continue* (General and Characters) carries on the last reply from where it stopped. *Styles* can now be copied and edited ("Edit styles…" under Style); the four built-in ones stay fixed.
  - The model picker labels and lists first the default model for the mode you are in.
  - `tools/walkthrough.ps1` can now fill in Windows open/save dialogs, so import and export are tested in the real app.
- **How M3 part 1 behaves, decided while building it:**
  - *Continue is hidden on a reply that has only "thinking" text* (stopped before any answer): the raw continuation cannot keep thinking apart from the answer.
  - *Continue changes the reply in place* rather than adding a version beside it: it is the same reply made longer. The raw prompt (template up to the reply, then its text so far) goes to the engine's `/completion`, so the model carries on mid-sentence.
  - *Greeting first:* a character chat's prompt starts with a short user turn, "[Start a new chat]", before the greeting, because strict chat templates (Mistral `[INST]`, Gemma) refuse a conversation that opens with a reply. Tried with real models using the ChatML and Llama 3 templates, which accept it; no model with a strict template was on this PC to try.
  - *Names in greetings are filled in when the chat starts.* Switching persona later changes what the model is told, but greetings already shown keep the old name.
  - *The card's "final instruction"* (post-history instructions) went at the end of the system message in part 1; since part 2 it goes with the newest user message.
  - *Pictures* are converted to PNG on import (so any picture can carry a card on export) and a 256-pixel avatar is cut from the top-centre square. There is no manual crop yet.
  - *No database links* from chats to characters and personas: adding them would make SQLite rebuild the chats table when upgrading. The store deletes a character's chats and clears a deleted persona itself. Upgrading an M2 database was tested.
- **Left out of M3 part 1, still owed:** everything listed under Next. Not tried live: a card with a JPEG or WebP picture (the code converts them through Windows, but only PNG cards were imported in the app).
- **What M2 delivered:** a Models section with five parts. *Recommended* shows researched picks per use case with a fit badge and the best file for this PC pre-selected. *Browse* searches Hugging Face, shows each repository's files with fit badges and the model card. *Library* lists models from the models folder and any added folders, and can load, benchmark, delete, or make one the default per mode. *Downloads* queues downloads, pauses and resumes them, checks free space first and verifies every file's SHA-256 before using it. *Engine* installs and updates DOG.ai's own CUDA build of llama.cpp. The side panel shows hardware, graphics memory, the context size used for fit, and an optional Hugging Face token stored with Windows DPAPI. The status bar now shows tokens per second and graphics memory, and an unused model unloads itself after 30 minutes.
- **How M2 behaves, decided while building it:**
  - *Going online* is a button in the Models section, off at start. Leaving the section goes offline, unless a download or engine install is running; then it goes offline when that finishes. "Go offline" pauses running downloads.
  - *Fit* adds the model file, the memory for the chosen context (read from the model's header where possible), and working memory, then compares with free graphics memory. Memory held by the currently loaded model counts as free, since loading another model releases it. A partial fit gives a layer count, which loading now uses instead of "all layers".
  - *Too large* models are refused before anything is unloaded, with a message saying how much memory they need and how much is free. A benchmark's proven settings override the estimate for the same context size. If the header cannot be read, the model is loaded anyway and the engine decides.
  - *Benchmark* loads the model with the estimated layer count, steps down if loading fails, measures, and saves the layers and speed for later loads. It tunes GPU layers only; it does not change the context size.
  - *Stable engine releases*: llama.cpp now marks numbered builds as pre-releases and publishes a stable release that names one build (b12110 on 2026-11-12). DOG.ai installs that build, picking the newest CUDA version the driver supports (CUDA 13.4 here) plus its runtime.
  - *Curated picks* are in `src/DogAI.Core/Models/curated-models.json` (researched 2026-11-12). There is no "refresh" button yet: the only allowed hosts are Hugging Face and GitHub, and there is no online copy of the list. Publishing the JSON in a GitHub repo would make it refreshable. Uncensored role-play fine-tunes were left out of the defaults; they can be found through Browse. Voice picks come with M6.
- **Left out of M2, still owed:**
  - Per-mode defaults are saved and the model picker labels the current mode's default (General, Characters and, since M4, Story). Nothing loads a default automatically yet.
  - ~~Links in model cards do not open.~~ Done in M7: every web link asks first, then opens in the user's browser.
  - Not tried live: a gated model with a token, partial offload of a model bigger than the card, and the 30-minute idle unload. Unit tests cover the logic.
  - The download resume and checksum tests use an in-process fake server, not a real local web server as the Verification section suggests.
  - Model cards written in HTML have their tags removed (the text stays) so they read cleanly; images show as links, since remote images are blocked.
  - The real data folder still uses the winget engine until Install is clicked once in Models → Engine.
- **What M1 delivered:** the app starts and stops `llama-server` itself (found from winget for now), loads any GGUF from the models folder or picked by hand, and General mode has streaming chat with stop, regenerate, edit-and-branch, version switching, markdown with code copy buttons, collapsed "thinking", four style presets, per-chat instructions, a context meter with automatic trimming of old turns, and a prompt inspector. Chats are saved in SQLite as a message tree.
- **Left out of M1 on purpose, still owed by a later milestone:**
  - ~~"Continue" a stopped reply, and editing your own sampler presets.~~ Done in M3 part 1.
  - ~~Syntax colouring and LaTeX in replies.~~ Done in M7 (highlight.js and KaTeX bundled under `DogAI.UI/wwwroot/vendor`).
  - ~~Idle auto-unload, VRAM readout and GPU-layer tuning.~~ Done in M2.
  - ~~The winget `llama-server` is a Vulkan build.~~ M2's Engine tab installs the CUDA build.
- **Decided after this plan was first written:**
  - The app ships **three switchable looks** instead of one: Workbench (default), Fireside and Playhouse, each in light and dark, chosen in Settings. See `CLAUDE.md` for how they are built. The "Console" proposal was dropped.
  - The logo and Windows icon are the **D-Dog** mark (`src/DogAI.App/Assets/icon.svg`).
  - Every new screen is checked in all six look/theme combinations as part of its milestone.
  - Original mockups are kept for reference in `design/proposals.html` and `design/icons.html`. Model names in them are placeholders.

## Context

You want a private, almost fully offline Windows desktop app for running local LLMs, maintainable in VS 2022, with three distinct modes (General, Character chat, Story) plus one online section for browsing and downloading models with hardware-aware recommendations.

`C:\Claude\DOG-AI` is empty and not a git repo, so this is a greenfield build.

**Your machine (measured):** RTX 4090 (24 GB VRAM), Core Ultra 9 285K (24 cores), 32 GB RAM, VS 2022 Pro 17.14 with the .NET desktop workload, .NET SDK 9.0.320. `D:` is empty (931 GB free); `C:` has 184 GB free.

**Decisions you made:** WPF + Blazor Hybrid UI; app-managed `llama-server` engine; extras = chat with documents, voice in/out. Image understanding and image generation are out of scope. *(Changed 2026-11-13: image generation is now planned as M9.)*

**Decisions I made (say so if you disagree):**
- **Target `net9.0-windows`** (since M8: `net9.0-windows10.0.19041.0` for the app, the same .NET 9 with access to Windows Hello). It is the newest framework VS 2022 supports. .NET 10 needs VS 2026; the code will move over with a one-line change if you upgrade.
- **Models live in `D:\DOG-AI\Models`** by default (configurable). App data (database, cards, settings) in `%LOCALAPPDATA%\DOG.ai`.
- **Two online touchpoints, not one:** Hugging Face (models, voices) and GitHub releases of llama.cpp (engine updates). Both live in the same Models section and nothing else in the app can reach the network.

## Architecture

Solution `DogAI.sln`, seven projects:

| Project | Role |
|---|---|
| `src/DogAI.App` | WPF host window, `BlazorWebView`, DI startup, tray/hotkeys, WebView2 lockdown |
| `src/DogAI.UI` | Razor class library: pages, components, CSS, bundled JS (highlight.js, KaTeX), no CDN |
| `src/DogAI.Core` | Domain models and logic with no UI or I/O dependencies: prompt builder, context budgeter, card parsing, lorebook matching, memory, story engine |
| `src/DogAI.Engine` | `llama-server` process manager, streaming client, GGUF header parser, hardware probe, fit estimator, voice engines |
| `src/DogAI.Data` | EF Core + SQLite, migrations, repositories, FTS5 search, vector storage |
| `src/DogAI.Hub` | **The only assembly allowed to open outbound connections**: Hugging Face client, download manager, engine updater |
| `tests/DogAI.Tests` | xUnit tests |

**Engine.** One `llama-server` child process per loaded model, bound to `127.0.0.1` on a random port, plus a small second instance for embeddings. The app talks to it over its HTTP API: streaming chat completions, `/tokenize` for exact token counts, `/apply-template` + `/completion` for continue/prefill, and JSON-schema-constrained output for structured results. Chat templates come from the GGUF itself. Idle auto-unload frees VRAM. On first run the app can adopt the `llama-server` you already have from winget until its own copy is downloaded.

**Offline guarantee (enforced, not just promised).**
- All `HttpClient` use sits behind one gateway in `DogAI.Hub`, gated by an "Online" switch that is off by default and only turns on inside the Models section.
- WebView2 is restricted to the app's own origin; any other request is blocked and logged. Links open in your browser only after a confirmation.
- Imported card and document text is treated as untrusted: markdown renders with raw HTML disabled.
- No telemetry, no update checks outside the Models section.

**Data.** SQLite. Messages are stored as a tree (each has a parent), which gives regenerate, swipes, edit-and-branch, and CYOA branching from one mechanism. Embeddings are stored as blobs with in-memory cosine search, which is plenty for single-user scale.

## Features

### Shared across all modes
- Streaming replies with stop, regenerate, continue, edit any message, branch from any point
- Sampler presets (temperature, min-p, top-p/k, DRY, XTC, repetition penalty), per mode and per character
- **Context budgeter**: exact token counting with priority trimming (system > card > lore > memory > recent turns) and a live context meter
- **Prompt inspector**: view the exact text sent to the model for any reply
- Collapsed display of reasoning ("thinking") output for reasoning models
- Tokens/sec and VRAM readout; quick model switcher in the title bar
- Full-text search across all chats and stories; backup/restore to a single archive; dark and light themes

### 1. General mode
- Assistants: reusable system-prompt profiles (coder, editor, tutor, etc.) with their own model and preset
- Markdown, syntax-highlighted code with copy button, LaTeX, tables
- Folders, pinning, auto-titling, export to Markdown
- Prompt library with fill-in variables
- **Chat with documents:** drop PDF, DOCX, text, markdown, or code files into a chat or a reusable "collection". Local embedding model finds relevant passages; answers cite the source passage, which opens on click.

### 2. Character mode
- **Import** Character Card V2 and V3 from PNG (embedded metadata) and JSON, including embedded lorebooks and alternate greetings. **Export** back to PNG/JSON.
- **Card editor** with all standard fields, avatar cropping, tags, and a live token count
- **AI card builder**: describe a character in a sentence and the loaded model drafts the full card for you to edit
- User personas (who *you* are in the chat), switchable per chat
- Lorebooks / world info: keyword-triggered entries with a token budget, attachable to characters or chats
- Swipes on every reply, alternate greetings, author's note, impersonate (model drafts your turn)
- **Long-term memory**: rolling summary of older turns plus vector recall of relevant past messages, so long chats stay coherent past the context window
- Group chats with multiple characters and turn-order control
- Per-character voice for read-aloud

### 3. Story mode
- **Story Bible**: premise, genre, tone, POV, tense, cast, world facts, style guide. Always in context and editable mid-story. Can be AI-drafted from a one-line idea.
- **Authored stories**: outline → chapters → scene beats; "write next" with optional steering; select text to rewrite, expand, shorten, or change tone
- **Interactive (CYOA)**:
  - Narrator turns end in 2–4 choices produced by schema-constrained output, so choices are always well-formed, plus a free-text "do something else"
  - Optional tracked state (inventory, stats, location, flags) the model updates each turn and you can edit
  - Optional dice checks for outcomes
  - **Branch map**: a visual tree of every path taken; click any node to rewind or explore an alternative
- Hierarchical summaries (scene → chapter → story so far) to keep long stories consistent
- Lorebooks and document collections usable as world reference
- Narrator read-aloud
- Export to Markdown, HTML, and EPUB

### 4. Models section (the online part)
- **Hardware profile**: GPU, VRAM (total and currently free), RAM, CPU, disk space per drive
- **Browse and search** Hugging Face GGUF repos with sort by trending/downloads/recent, model card view, and per-file listing
- **Fit badge on every file**, computed from file size, layer count, and the context length you want:
  - Fits fully on GPU (fast)
  - Partial offload (slower, with an estimate)
  - Too large
- Per repo, the best quantization for your hardware is pre-selected
- **Curated picks** per use case (General, Roleplay, Story, Embedding, Voice) for your VRAM tier. Shipped as a JSON file, refreshable when online. I'll research the current model landscape when building this list rather than rely on memory.
- **Download manager**: queue, pause/resume, SHA-256 verification, free-space check, optional Hugging Face token for gated models (stored encrypted with Windows DPAPI)
- **Library**: installed models with metadata read from the GGUF, add existing GGUF folders, delete, set defaults per mode
- **Benchmark** after download: measures real tokens/sec and auto-tunes GPU layers and context size
- **Engine updater**: installs/updates the CUDA build of llama.cpp

### Voice
- **Speech-to-text**: Whisper via `Whisper.net`, push-to-talk and hands-free
- **Text-to-speech**: neural voices (Piper/Kokoro) via `sherpa-onnx`, spoken sentence by sentence as the reply streams; Windows built-in voices as a zero-download fallback
- Audio via `NAudio`. Voice models download through the Models section.

## Design gate (completed; outcome is in Status above)

No styling, navigation, or page layout gets built until you have picked a look. This happens in the middle of M0, right after the bare shell proves the WPF + Blazor pipeline runs.

- I produce **four distinct design directions** as clickable HTML mockups. Because the app's UI is HTML/CSS, the chosen mockup carries straight over into the real app.
- Each direction differs in both **aesthetic** (palette, typography, density, how much "DOG" personality the branding has) and **layout** (for example: left icon rail with mode workspaces, top tabs, a three-pane layout with a persistent context panel, a home-screen launcher that opens each mode full-window).
- Each mockup shows the same four screens so they compare fairly: General chat, Character chat, Story (CYOA turn with choices and branch map), and the Models browser with fit badges. Dark and light variants of each.
- You pick one, or mix parts ("layout of B, colours of D"). I do one revision round on the pick.
- The result is frozen as a set of design tokens (CSS variables) and a shell layout in `DogAI.UI`, which every later milestone builds on. Later screens that the mockups did not cover get a quick mockup for your approval before they are built.

## M17: Image understanding (planned and approved by the owner 2026-11-15)

**What it delivers.** The chat model can look at a picture.

1. **A picture in a chat message.** In General, character chats and group chats the chat box takes pictures (a button, paste, or a file dropped on it); the model answers about what it sees, and the picture stays in the chat with its message.
2. **A prompt from a picture.** In Images, **Write it for me…** can start from a picture in place of a sentence: the chat model looks at it and writes a prompt, checked against the tag list as today. The picture is only looked at; nothing is drawn from it (image-to-image stays out).
3. **Models knows which chat models can see.** A "Sees pictures" mark on Recommended, Browse and On this PC › Chat models; the extra file such a model needs is downloaded with it.
4. **Three small things that ride along:** an Illustrate button on replies in General chats (character and group chats have one); the owner's decision on LoRA file words (Status, 2026-11-15); and two checks left open by M15 and M16.

**How picture input works, and what is on this PC (looked at 2026-11-15).** The chat engine can load a second file beside a chat model, its *projector* (a GGUF with "mmproj" in its name, made for one model family). With it, a message can carry pictures. The chat engine on this PC has the switch (`--mmproj`). **None of the five chat models in `D:\DOG.ai\Models` has a projector file**, so nothing here can see a picture today: step 1 needs a download, either a projector for a model already here (if one exists and works with the uncensored versions) or a model that comes with one. DOG.ai hides projector files from its lists on purpose (`Quantization.IsAddOn`) and never passes one to the engine; `PromptMessage` is text only; messages have nowhere to keep a picture.

**Settled already** (follows from earlier milestones, not up for choice):

- A picture goes only to the chat engine on `127.0.0.1`. Nothing new goes online; the downloads are ordinary model downloads through the Models section.
- An attached picture is kept only in the database (so under the lock, in backups, moved with a portable folder), never as a file, a cache or in TEMP. It is deleted with its message and leaves the disk the way removed data does.
- A file from outside is untrusted. It is opened only by the page (the browser control), reduced and written again there as a plain picture, so what DOG.ai keeps has no camera, place or date information, and no code of DOG.ai's own reads a foreign picture file. Anything that is not a plain picture (SVG included) is refused. The owner's file is never changed, moved or copied.
- Pictures and what the model says about them are never written to the log.
- All three looks, dark and light; ids for every new control; `CLAUDE.md` gets "How image understanding works".

**Defaults chosen here (say so to change one):**

1. **Where:** General, character chats and group chats (they share the chat box). Not stories.
2. **How many and how large:** up to four pictures a message, each reduced to what the model reads (the trial gives the number; about 1,024 to 1,536 pixels on the long side).
3. **The button shows only when it can work**, as the voice buttons do: when the chat's model can see pictures. Pasting a picture with a model that cannot shows one line saying so and where such models are marked.
4. **Older pictures:** a picture stays in what the model is given for as long as its message does (the oldest messages are left out first when room runs out, as today). The context meter counts it. With a model that cannot see, the picture is replaced by a short note for the model, and the message says "The model could not see this picture".
5. **Text-only jobs stay text-only:** summaries, recalled messages, search, the markdown export and a story made from a chat get "[picture]" where a picture was, never the picture.
6. **A prompt from a picture uses the default model for General**, as Write it for me does. If that model cannot see, the dialog says so.

**Steps, and which model to use for each.** One step per session, each ending in a build, passing tests, a look at the app, this plan's Status updated and a commit, then the hand-over.

| # | Step | Delivers | Model / effort |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | **Trial** (no app code) | By hand, outside DOG.ai, in a scratch folder. Which model: candidates looked up on Hugging Face then; **the owner is asked before each download, with its name and size**. For each: does it load with its projector on the chat engine this PC has and on the one DOG.ai installs; does a projector made for the original model work with the owner's uncensored versions; graphics memory the projector adds; seconds to read a picture; how many tokens a picture takes at several sizes and whether that can be capped; which file formats the engine reads (kept pictures are WebP); several pictures in one message and over several turns, and whether a later turn reads them again; with thinking off and with a fixed answer form (the Images helper needs both); counting tokens and showing the prompt when a picture is in it; what happens when a picture is sent to a model without a projector; what the engine prints and writes to disk when given a picture. How well it sees: a photo, a screenshot with text, and three kept pictures turned into tags and compared with their own prompts. Ends with the model to recommend, the numbers for the budget and for fit, the open points settled, and steps 2 to 6 corrected in this plan. | Fable 5.2, high |
| 2 | **Core, storage and engine** | Pictures on a message: a table of their own (not `Pictures`, whose rows are drawn pictures with their settings), the picture as its last column, deleted with the message, with its migration; edit, regenerate and swipes keep the question's pictures. The prompt: `PromptMessage` carries pictures, `LlamaApi` sends them, the budget counts them (the trial's number), the prompt inspector shows a marker, default 5 above. The engine: a model's projector found by rule in the model's own folder (no saved full path, so `PortableMove` needs nothing), given to the engine on load, `IChatEngine` says whether the loaded model sees; fit, "Fits beside pictures" and the measured memory count the projector; a projector that is missing or refused loads the model without it and says so. **LoRA file words sent cleaned** (the picture maker and the test picture; `CLAUDE.md`'s "Not cleaned on purpose" changed). Tests with the fake engine and the real database; a real-engine test behind an environment variable; lock, backup, restore and portable checks for the new table; a deleted message's picture gone from the data folder. **In the app: a locked data folder made by the previous build, opened by the new one** (the check left open since M16 step 1; this step brings a migration of its own). No screen yet. | Fable 5.2, high |
| 3 | **Models** | "Sees pictures" on Recommended, Browse and On this PC › Chat models. A model with a projector in its repository is offered with it ("with picture input, + 0.9 GB") and both land in one folder; a model here without its projector offers **Get picture input**; Delete removes both. The trial's pick in the Recommended list. Fit badges count the projector. A note in the defaults block when the General default cannot see. All three looks, dark and light. | Opus 5.5, high |
| 4 | **Chats** | The chat box: attach button, paste, drop; small previews above the box with a remove button each; reducing and rewriting in the page. Pictures shown with their message, a large view on a click. The notes of defaults 3 and 4; the context meter. Editing a message keeps or removes its pictures. General, a character chat and a group chat, each with a real model answering about real pictures. All three looks, dark and light, at 700, 1000 and 1400 px. | Opus 5.5, high |
| 5 | **Images and General** | **Write it for me… › From a picture**: the picture shown on the Make tab, a kept picture, or a file; the model fills the same form as today (how many people, then tags; no thinking; tags looked up; the rating from Settings › Pictures; no quality or trigger words), and the result goes to Add after the prompt / Replace the prompt as today. The picture is held in memory only, like the helper's sentence. The card: the chat model comes back through `EnsureReadyAsync` while the picture model may be loaded. **Illustrate on replies in General chats** (the planner with nobody's Looks). All three looks, dark and light. | Opus 5.5, high |
| 6 | **Finish** | Leak check as in M16 step 6, with pictures in place of words: pictures attached and sent, attached and removed before sending, a message with a picture deleted, a chat deleted, a file used for From a picture; pieces of each searched for as bytes and as base64 in the data folder, models folder, engine folders and TEMP, plain and locked. The published portable build. `/code-review high`. Every new screen in three looks, dark and light, and narrow. **Unload everything while a chat model is loading and during a benchmark** (left open since M15). `CLAUDE.md` ("How image understanding works"), this plan's Status. | Fable 5.2, xhigh |

Why this split: steps 1, 2 and 6 are where a wrong judgement costs most (what the engine can really do, private pictures on disk and a migration, the last look). Steps 3 to 5 repeat patterns the code has (the "Fits beside pictures" chip and paired downloads, the picture maker's canvas code and `MessagePictures`, the prompt helper).

**Checks that decide whether it is done:** a real model answers correctly about a photo, a screenshot with text and a kept picture, in each of the three kinds of chat; a chat with pictures survives lock, backup and restore, and a portable move; a deleted picture is nowhere on disk; a chat model without a projector behaves exactly as before M17 (same prompt, same memory, same speed); a picture in a chat makes the context meter move by what the engine then reports; From a picture on a kept picture gives tags a person would agree with.

**Not in M17 (could follow):** pictures in stories; drafting a character's Looks from their portrait; reading scanned pages for Documents with the model; video and sound as input; anything that draws from a picture. The other points under "Not checked" in Status (an upscaler that enlarges ×2, a sentence checkpoint, more than 420 kept pictures and the rest) stay open, as does unsaved typing in the lore editor, personas and Assistants and prompts.

## M18: Smaller pictures, settings in pictures, thinking budget (planned 2026-11-15)

The owner's three requests (2026-11-15), in the owner's words: (1) "Images should be defaulted to a lower resolution. It may be expanded on manually." (2) "When in character or story chat, the tags generated should try and pull all the detail about the background as well as the character. For example, if the story is in a forest, those tags should be used so the image doesn't show an indoor scene." (3) "I believe thinking is important to the character and story mode, but we have to have options to dial it back if it takes too long. Can there be a throttle on the thinking?"

M18 does not depend on M17 and M17 does not depend on it: the owner may build either first. Building M18 first gives M17's "Illustrate in General" the better place words for free.

### 1. Pictures start smaller, made larger by hand

**Today.** Every picture starts at about one megapixel: Tall 832 × 1216, Square 1024 × 1024, Wide 1216 × 832 (`PictureLevels`), in the Images section (`ImagesControls.StartFor`), the picture maker (portraits, story and chat pictures) and the test picture. Draft makes fewer steps, not a smaller picture. Larger already exists: Fine (hires fix), and in Images Upscale › Redraw larger (same seed, same layout).

**What changes.**

- **A size choice: Small or Normal**, beside shape and quality. Small is the new default everywhere a picture is started; Normal is today's size. **Small is 640 × 960 tall, 960 × 640 wide, 768 × 768 square** (about 0.6 megapixels; chosen by looking in the trial: one step smaller breaks faces and hands). Every side is a multiple of 64, because the picture engine rounds up to one.
- **Making it larger by hand:** every try has **Make larger**, which redraws it from the same seed with a hires fix, so the layout stays and only the detail grows: **Small ×1.5 gives 960 × 1472** (wide 1472 × 960, square 1152 × 1152) with Fine's own second pass (half the steps, strength 0.3, Lanczos); Normal → Fine's size as today. In Images it is the existing Upscale › Redraw larger offered from a Small try; in the picture maker it is a button beside Try again. Typing a width and height in the Images side panel stays as it is. The same seed at another size is another picture (seen in the trial), so Make larger never simply draws at the larger size.
- **Fits beside the chat model** (`PictureMemory.FitsBeside`; simpler than first planned, since Small is the start everywhere): (a) when Settings says Normal and a Normal picture would set the loaded chat model aside where a Small one would not, the picture starts Small and the note says so; (b) Make larger uses ×1.5, or ×1.25 (exactly Normal's size, a little softer) when only that fits beside the chat model: with Huihui Q8 loaded ×1.5 needs the chat model set aside and ×1.25 does not. If even Small does not fit, it is drawn as today (the chat model set aside) and the note says so.
- **Settings › Pictures** gets "Start pictures at: Small / Normal" (`PicturePreferences`); the Images section's remembered size wins there once the owner has chosen one.

**Must not change.** A kept picture redraws byte for byte (its size is in its row). `PictureTries.Adopt` / `BaseOf` tell a kept picture's size from its row as they tell quality from steps and hires fix; Try again on a kept Normal picture stays Normal. `ImageRequest.TiledAbovePixels` stays where it is (a Small picture made larger is 1.41 megapixels, well under it). A Small picture made larger is a row with a hires fix at 640 × 960: `BaseOf` must not take it for Fine (Fine is a hires fix at Normal's size), so size is read from the row's width and height first.

### 2. Chat and story pictures take their place from the setting

**Today (the cause).** The scene planner (`PictureWriter.SceneAsync`) gets the story's premise and World box under the heading **"Background, for understanding only (do not draw it)"** (`Illustrations.Background`). A passage that never says "forest" leaves the planner guessing the place, and it often guesses indoors. Character and group chats pass only up to six earlier messages (`ChatPictures.Background`); the character's Scenario reaches the planner only inside "Who these people are… use it for what they wear" (`PictureWriter.About`). The model gives at most four place words and `PicturePrompt` keeps three.

**The cause, corrected by the trial.** The heading is not it: when the World box names the place, today's planner draws it (forest, ship's deck, rainy city at night, tavern: every run), even for a passage that is only talk. Two other things are: (1) **the place was set only in earlier passages**, which the planner never sees for a story (it gets the premise and World box and nothing of what came before); (2) **in chats the Scenario is lost when the card's description is long**: it rides at the end of "who these people are", which is cut at 500 characters (`PictureWriter.MaxCastAboutLength`), after the description and personality. And with no place words in the prompt, the checkpoint draws a room.

**What changes** (in this order of worth).

- **What happened just before, for stories:** the planner is given the end of the passage before (as chats already pass up to six earlier messages), under "What happened just before (it tells where they are)". In the trial this alone turned a tavern and a bedroom into the forest campfire, 3 of 3.
- **Where the last picture was** is remembered with that picture (its place and light words, in the message's story details beside `picture`, through `StoryTurn`; no new table). When the newest passage does not say where it is, the planner is given "The last picture of this story took place in: forest, night, campfire. Stay there unless the passage or the setting says otherwise." (3 of 3 in the trial.) It covers a run of talk-only passages that the passage before does not.
- **The Scenario gets its own block, in full:** character chats pass the card's Scenario (and the chat's own summary) as the setting, no longer inside the 500-character "about"; group chats each member's Scenario. Stories pass the World box and premise under the same heading: "Setting: where the story takes place. It is to be drawn: take the place, the light and the weather from it, unless the passage clearly says this moment is somewhere else." (Trial: beach and dawn 3 of 3 where today gave a shore at sunset or under cloud.) The planner cuts its background at 1,500 characters today; the setting, what came before and the last place each need room of their own.
- **More place words: up to five kept** (the model may write six), three for light, checked against the tag list as today. The place line of the form says: the place itself first, then indoors or outdoors, then what is seen there; and take it from the setting when the passage does not say. Six in the trial gave "outdoors" or "indoors" almost every time and real detail (moss, fern, pebble), and in a drawn comparison did not crowd out the two people; it also gave things that are not places (mug, chart, potted plant) and repeated light words as place words, so code drops a place word that is also a light word. To watch in step 3: with six words the planner twice put a second person into a passage that had one.
- **The place shown before drawing:** when "Show me the prompt before drawing" is on, the review shows the place and light words on a line of their own, easy to correct.

**Must not change.** The owner's Looks are put in as typed; at most two people; the rating rules; nothing the planner reads is logged.

### 3. A thinking budget

**Today.** Thinking is on or off per kind of chat: General on, character and group chats off, stories off (`ChatOptions`, `StoryOptions`; owner's decision 2026-11-15). With it on, Huihui Qwen3.5 9B thinks for 2,500 to 4,000 tokens before answering, and a long think can use up the room and leave no answer (`ThinkingOnly`).

**What the engine offers (looked at 2026-11-15).** The chat engine on this PC (llama.cpp build 12314) has `--reasoning-budget N` (a limit on thinking tokens; -1 for none, 0 for none at all) and `--reasoning-budget-message` (words put in just before thinking is ended, e.g. "Time to answer."), plus `--reasoning-effort`. **Settled by the trial: the budget is given per request, on both engines** (the winget one, build 12314, and the one DOG.ai installed, build 12359). The request body takes `thinking_budget_tokens` (a number of thinking tokens; 0 is no thinking; left out is no limit) and, if wanted, `reasoning_budget_message`; nothing is reloaded. Both engines also end the thinking of a reply in progress on request: the reply is asked for with `reasoning_control: true`, and `POST /v1/chat/completions/control` with `{ "id": <the reply's id from the stream>, "action": "reasoning_end" }` makes the model answer at once. DOG.ai's own route (stop the request, ask again with the thinking so far closed) was tried and works too (the answer began 0.2 s into the second request), but it is not needed and is not built.

**What changes.**

- **A thinking level** in place of each on/off switch, for General, character and group chats, and stories: **Off, Short, Normal, Long, No limit**. The trial confirmed the token counts: **Short 512** (a reply in about 8 s), **Normal 1,536** (about 20 s), **Long 4,096** (up to about 50 s); Off stays the template switch it is today. Starting levels (owner's decision, 2026-11-15): General No limit; character chats, group chats and stories **Short**. On the first start after M18 the old switches are read once: General on → No limit, General off → Off; character and group chats and stories → Short whatever their switch said (they were off by default, so "off" cannot be told apart from untouched). Backups made before M18 restore the same way. Summaries and DOG.ai's own jobs still never think.
- **Answer now:** while a reply is thinking, a button beside Stop ends the thinking at once and makes the model answer, through the engine's own control route (every chat reply is asked for with `reasoning_control: true`; `LlamaApi` keeps the reply's id from the stream). Stop still stops everything.
- **A count while it thinks:** "Thinking… 1,200 / 1,536" in the reply as it streams. DOG.ai counts the thinking pieces itself (one piece is one token): the engine reports no thinking count of its own.
- **Notes:** a reply whose thinking was cut by the budget or by Answer now shows that in small type under its thinking. The engine puts **no mark in the stream** at a cut (the thinking simply ends and the answer begins), so "cut by the budget" is told from the count: a 512 budget ended at 489 to 493 pieces, 1,536 at 1,487 to 1,504, 4,096 at 4,013 (within about 100 of the budget). `ThinkingOnly` keeps telling stopped, out of room and ended by itself apart, and learns "cut by the budget" (no answer after the cut would be a model that ignores the closing).
- **No budget message.** The engine can add words at the cut ("I will write the reply now."); the trial saw no gain in chats and stories, and on a sums question the reply with the message was wrong where the plain cut was right (one sample). The thinking stays the model's own words.
- **An engine that does not know the fields** ignores them (an unknown field is not refused), so the thinking runs past the budget and Answer now gets no "success". DOG.ai then says under the reply that the chat engine needs updating in Models › Engines for thinking limits; nothing else is built for it.

### Decided here (say so to change one)

1. Small becomes the default for every new picture: Images, portraits, story and chat pictures, and the test picture.
2. The place in a picture follows the story's world and the character's setting unless the passage says otherwise.
3. **Thinking levels start at:** General No limit; character chats, group chats and stories Short (owner's decision, 2026-11-15: "thinking is important to the character and story mode").
4. *From the trial (2026-11-15):* Small is 640 × 960 and Make larger is ×1.5, or ×1.25 when only that fits beside the loaded chat model (**both confirmed by the owner, 2026-11-15**); not yet asked of the owner: no words are added at a thinking cut; an engine too old for the thinking fields gets a note to update it and no route of DOG.ai's own.
5. The Images section forgets a size remembered before M18 once (owner's decision, 2026-11-15): every save there wrote the size, chosen or not.

### Trial results (step 1, 2026-11-15)

Run by hand, outside DOG.ai, with hard links in a throwaway folder on D: (removed afterwards; the owner's models and engine folders are byte for byte as before). Chat model: Huihui Qwen3.5 9B abliterated Q8, about 85 tokens a second. Checkpoint: `illustrious_novaFurryXL_ilV180A` with the Illustrious defaults (Euler, 28 steps, guidance 5.5). Picture engine `master-1104-5dcfa79`. Three scratch programs (thinking in Python against the engine's own address; sizes and place in C# against `DogAI.Core` and `DogAI.Engine`); none is in the repo.

**Sizes** (104 pictures drawn; 14 contact sheets looked at, with crops of faces and hands).

| Size (tall) | Megapixels | Seconds | Card at the peak, above the idle picture model | Look |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 576 × 864 (drawn as 576 × 896) | 0.52 | 3.4 | about 1.8 GB | Layout, place and two people fine; on full-body pictures a smeared eye and a hand with extra fingers. Too small. |
| **640 × 960** | 0.61 | 4.0 | 2.2 GB | Good: faces a little simpler on a full-body picture, hands right, two people kept apart with the right hair and clothes, places as good as larger. |
| 704 × 1056 (drawn as 704 × 1088) | 0.77 | 4.8 | 2.7 GB | Good, hard to tell from Normal; saves little. |
| 832 × 1216 (Normal) | 1.01 | 6.6 | 3.6 GB | Today's picture. |

- Wide and square sizes of the same area took the same seconds and memory and looked the same (864 × 576, 960 × 640, 1056 × 704, 1216 × 832; 704, 768, 864, 1024 square).
- **The engine rounds each side up to a multiple of 64** (576 × 864 came out 576 × 896, 864 square came out 896). Small's three sizes are multiples of 64, so they come out as asked.
- The idle picture model holds 7.0 GB. With Windows' own 1.6 GB the card peaked at 11.0 GB for Small and 12.5 GB for Normal (GB here are gibibytes, as in the app). `PictureMemory.Need` says 10.5 and 12.1 GB, about 1 GB above what was measured (9.5 and 10.9): left as it is.
- **The same seed at another size is another picture** (other pose, other framing), so a Small picture cannot be "drawn again at Normal": Make larger has to be the hires fix.
- **Made larger** (Small with Fine's second pass: 14 steps, strength 0.3, Lanczos; the whole redraw from the seed):

| | Comes out | Seconds | Card at the peak (with Windows) | Look |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| ×1.25 | 832 × 1216 (exactly Normal) | 7.4 | 12.5 to 13.2 GB | Same layout; sharper than Small, a little softer than a picture drawn at Normal. |
| ×1.3 | 832 × 1280 | 7.6 | 12.8 to 13.4 GB | An odd shape (the engine's rounding); dropped. |
| **×1.5** | 960 × 1472 | 8.7 | 14.2 to 14.6 GB | Same layout; as good as or better than a picture drawn at Normal. Small things change as with Fine (a mouth opened). |
| Normal, drawn plain | 832 × 1216 | 6.6 | 12.5 to 13.2 GB | (another picture: other pose) |
| Fine | 1280 × 1856 | 15.4 | 18.1 GB | Today's Fine. |

- **Beside the chat model** (Huihui Q8 at 8,192 tokens holds 8.6 GB; drawn for real with both loaded): Small peaked at 19.5 GB of 24.0 and Normal at 21.1 GB, same seconds as alone, and the chat model answered afterwards at its usual speed. By DOG.ai's rule (need + 1 GB margin against 13.8 GB free) Small fits with 2.2 GB to spare and Normal with 0.65 GB: Normal is the one that tips over when the document search model or a speech model is also on the card, or with a chat model holding more than about 9.3 GB; Small fits up to about 10.9 GB. Small made larger ×1.5 (13.7 GB by the rule) does not fit beside Huihui Q8; ×1.25 does.

**Place** (the scene planner's form filled 132 times: 12 cases, three seeds, up to five wordings; 2 to 5 s each).

- **World box names the place, passage does not** (forest, ship, city at night, tavern; also a forest passage that is only talk): today's wording gave the right place every time (forest + fog/moss; ship deck; alley, street, night, rain, neon; tavern, candlelight). The new heading changed little.
- **Place only in the passage before, World box empty** (two people talk and read a letter; the passage before put them at a campfire in the forest at night): today → garden bench, tavern, bedroom. New wording alone → indoors 3 of 3. **With the passage before → forest, campfire, night 3 of 3. With the last picture's place → 3 of 3.**
- **Character chat, long card** (description of 650 characters, so the Scenario falls outside the 500 the planner reads): today → "rocky shore", "steps", light sunset or overcast (guessed from the word "tide"). **Scenario in its own block → beach, dawn 3 of 3.** With a short card today's wording already gave beach and dawn.
- **The passage names another place** (a woodcutter's cabin in the forest story, the captain's cabin on the ship, the lighthouse kitchen in the beach chat): the passage won every time, in every wording. With the new wording the light the passage does not give is taken from the setting (dawn in the kitchen).
- **Six place words against three:** six almost always include "outdoors" or "indoors" and add real detail (moss, fern, pebble, foam); they also bring things that are not places (mug, chart, potted plant, mushroom) and light words as place words (night, moonlight), and in one case two of three runs added a person the passage did not have. Drawn by hand at 960 × 640, two seeds: **no place words → a room, both times**; "forest, night, campfire" → the forest campfire; eight place and light words → the same with moss and ferns, the two people unharmed.

**Thinking** (both engines gave the same results; times are from the winget one at 16,384 tokens of context).

- **Body fields that work:** `thinking_budget_tokens` (and its other name `reasoning_budget_tokens`); `reasoning_budget_message`; `reasoning_control`. **Ignored without an error:** `reasoning_budget`, `thinking: { budget_tokens }`, `reasoning_effort` (this model's template has no effort levels).
- **A cut in the stream:** the `reasoning_content` pieces stop (after the message's words when one is set) and `content` begins; no field marks it, the finish reason is "stop" as always, and the usage figures do not count thinking apart.
- **The control route:** `{"success":true}` and the answer began 0.05 s later; without `reasoning_control` on the reply `{"success":false,"message":"reasoning control not enabled for this completion"}`; a finished or unknown id `{"success":false,"message":"no active completion for this id"}`; an unknown action is refused with 400.
- **DOG.ai's own route** (stop at 512 thinking pieces, ask again with an assistant turn that holds the thinking so far and `</think>`): the second request read about 550 tokens again and began its answer in 0.15 to 0.2 s; replies as good as the engine's own cut. Not needed.
- **Seconds** (whole reply; in brackets the thinking tokens):

| | No limit | 4,096 | 1,536 | 512 | Off |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Character chat, turn 1 | 33.6 (2,641) | 33.9 (2,641) | 20.2 (1,502) | 8.3 (492) | 2.7 |
| Character chat, turn 2 | 46.8 (3,779) | 46.8 (3,779) | 19.6 (1,504) | 7.8 (493) | 2.0 |
| Character chat, turn 3 | 35.1 (2,778) | 34.8 (2,778) | 19.6 (1,487) | 7.7 (492) | 2.4 |
| Story passage (about 200 words) | 82.7, **no passage** (6,846, stopped by the trial's limit of 7,000) | 51.6 (4,013) | 21.5 (1,497) | 9.6 (489) | 3.9 |

- The answer begins about 6 s after sending at 512, 18 s at 1,536, 48 s at 4,096. 4,096 is reached only by stories here.
- **After a cut the answers were good:** in character, in the asked form (two or three short paragraphs; a passage of about 200 words ending on a decision), and often the very draft the thinking had reached. Read side by side, 512 was not worse than no limit; with no limit the story model fell to counting the words of its draft and never wrote the passage.
- With the budget message the replies were no better; on a sums question the reply with the message gave a wrong number where the plain cut gave the steps and the right one (one sample).

### Steps, and which model to use for each

One step per session, each ending in a build, passing tests, a look at the app, this plan's Status updated and a commit, then the hand-over.

| # | Step | Delivers | Model / effort |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | **Trial** (no app code). **Done 2026-11-15**: see "Trial results" above | By hand, in a scratch folder, with hard links to the owner's models. **Sizes:** draw the same three prompts and seeds (one person tall, two people wide, a place with nobody) with the Illustrious checkpoint at 576 × 864, 640 × 960, 704 × 1056 and 832 × 1216, and the square and wide sizes of the same area; seconds, graphics memory, and a look at each (faces, hands, two people kept apart); then the chosen small size made larger (hires fix ×1.3 and ×1.5) beside a plain Normal picture: same layout, as good? Whether Small fits beside Huihui Q8 loaded where Normal does not. **Place:** `PictureWriterSmokeTests` (or a scratch program) with five passages that do not name their place (a forest story, a ship, a city at night, a tavern, a character chat whose Scenario sets the place), with today's wording and the new one: place words, whether the passage still wins when it does name a place, six place words against three. **Thinking:** on the winget llama-server and on the one DOG.ai installs, whether the budget and its message can be given per request (try the body fields the engine accepts; read its source for the names if needed), what a cut looks like in the stream, whether the answer after a cut is good; the fallback route (stop, ask again with the thinking closed) with Huihui Qwen3.5 9B; seconds saved at 512, 1,536 and 4,096 against no limit on three character-chat turns and one story passage. Ends with the numbers, the route for the budget, and steps 2 to 5 corrected here. | Fable 5.2, high |
| 2 | **Picture sizes**. **Done 2026-11-15** (see Status) | `PictureSize` (Small, Normal) in Core beside quality and shape: Small 640 × 960, 960 × 640, 768 × 768 (every side a multiple of 64; a test checks); `At(quality, shape, size)`; `Adopt`/`BaseOf` read the size from the row's width and height first, so a Small picture made larger (a hires fix at 640 × 960) is not taken for Fine; Make larger in the picture maker (Small: ×1.5 with Fine's second pass, or ×1.25 when only that fits beside the loaded chat model; Normal: Fine); the fits-beside rule (Normal in Settings but only Small fits beside the chat model: start Small and say so); Images: Small in `StartFor`, Make larger from a try (Redraw larger; `RedrawSizes` already offers ×1.5 of the size first drawn at); Settings › Pictures "Start pictures at"; the test picture. Tests: a kept Normal and a kept Fine picture still redraw byte for byte; Try again keeps the size; a Small picture made larger and kept comes back as Small made larger. In the app: a portrait, a story picture and Images, all three looks, dark and light; a Small picture and Make larger with Huihui Q8 loaded (the chat model stays for Small). | Opus 5.5, high |
| 3 | **Settings in pictures** | In the order the trial found worth: (1) stories pass the end of the passage before ("What happened just before"); (2) the last picture's place and light kept with it (`StoryTurn`) and passed on; (3) the Scenario in its own block, in full, no longer inside the 500-character "about" (character chats, with the chat's summary; group chats each member's), stories' World and premise under the same "Setting… it is to be drawn" heading, each block with room of its own beside the 1,500-character background; (4) the place line of the form (place first, then indoors or outdoors, then what is seen; from the setting when the passage does not say), up to five place words kept and three for light, a place word that is also a light word dropped; (5) the place line in the review. Tests with the fake engine for what the planner is given; the smoke test with the trial's cases: place only in the passage before (forest campfire), long card with a beach Scenario (beach, dawn), a passage that moves indoors (indoors), and that nobody the passage does not show is added. In the app: a story whose World box is empty, set in a forest by its first passage, a later talk-only passage illustrated (forest); a character chat with a long card whose Scenario sets the place. All three looks for the review. | Opus 5.5, high |
| 4 | **Thinking budget** | The levels in Settings › Chats and stories (`ChatOptions`, `StoryOptions`; old switches read once as described above, characters and stories to Short; backups); Short 512, Normal 1,536, Long 4,096 sent per request as `thinking_budget_tokens` (`SamplerPreset`, `[JsonIgnore]` like `MaxTokens`; `LlamaApi.ChatRequestBody`); no budget message; `reasoning_control: true` on chat replies, the reply's id kept from the stream, and Answer now through `POST /v1/chat/completions/control` (`reasoning_end`) on the loopback client, behind `IChatEngine`; the count while thinking (thinking pieces counted in DOG.ai); the notes and `ThinkingOnly` ("cut by the budget" told from the count, within about 100 of the budget); the note for an engine that ignores the fields. DOG.ai's own jobs and summaries still never think. Tests with a fake engine that thinks forever. In the app with Huihui Qwen3.5 9B, with the winget engine and the installed one: General, a character chat, a group chat and a story at Short and No limit, Answer now in each; a story at No limit that never answers, ended with Answer now; all three looks, dark and light. | Fable 5.2, high |
| 5 | **Finish** | `/code-review high` over M18; every changed screen in three looks, dark and light, and narrow; the published portable build; `CLAUDE.md` (sizes, the planner's setting, the thinking budget); this plan's Status. | Opus 5.5, high |

**Checks that decide whether it is done:** a new picture anywhere starts Small and Make larger keeps its layout; every kept picture still redraws byte for byte; a forest story's passage that does not say "forest" gets a forest picture, also when only an earlier passage said so; a chat with a long card gets its Scenario's place; a passage that moves indoors gets an indoor one; a reply at Short thinks no more than its budget and then answers; Answer now gives an answer within seconds; General chats behave exactly as before M18; character chats, group chats and stories think, within Short's budget, and still answer.

## M14 to M16: The owner's six ideas (planned 2026-11-15)

The owner's list, numbered in the order given:

1. Edit in the picture maker should let the owner keep or randomise the seed.
2. Clicking Settings a second time returns to the screen the owner was on (a chat, a story…); the same for Search.
3. A new section, Images, devoted to making pictures, with the feel of automatic1111.
4. An Unload button under "Graphics memory" that unloads all models.
5. Every model can be downloaded, even one that will not fit; models small enough to sit in memory beside the picture model are marked.
6. The document search model is unloaded when it is not being used.

Order: the two small ones first (M14), then the memory work (M15), because the Images section (M16) leans on both: it reuses the seed control and makes the card busier than any screen so far.

### M14: Quick fixes (ideas 1, 2)

1. **Seed in Edit and redraw.** Today Edit always keeps the seed and Try again always takes a new one (`PictureMaker.razor`). Edit gets a seed row: the number (editable), **Keep** (the default, as now) and **New seed**. Build it as a small shared component, since the Images section needs the same control. A kept picture redrawn with a new seed is a new try; the kept one is not touched until Use.
2. **Settings and Search go back.** The three shells link straight to `settings` and `search`. A small Core service remembers the last address that was neither; clicking the button while already there (any `settings/<category>`, or Search) returns to it, and with nothing remembered goes to the look's start page. The button's tooltip says "Back to …" while it would go back. Escape is left alone (dialogs and the drawer use it). Ctrl+K keeps working as now.

Checks: both in all three looks, dark and light; back from Settings into a chat, a group chat, a story, a character editor with unsaved typing (it must still be there, or the owner is warned as today), and Models.

### M15: Graphics memory and downloads (ideas 4, 5, 6)

1. **Unload everything** (`#unload-all`) under "Graphics memory" in the Models side panel: the chat model (through `ModelLauncher.UnloadByUserAsync`, so it is the owner's unload and nothing brings it back until asked), the picture model (`ImageServer`), the document search model (`EmbeddingServer`) and any speech model that holds graphics memory. It takes a card turn like every other change to the card; while a reply or a picture is being made it is disabled and says why. The readout updates afterwards.
2. **The document search model is let go sooner.** It already stops by itself, but only after 10 minutes unused (`EmbeddingServer.IdleStop`). New: 2 minutes, like the picture model; at once when the card needs the room (a chat model load or a picture that would not fit otherwise); and never while indexing or a search is running. The Documents side panel says whether it is in memory.
3. **Download anything.** `DownloadButton` is disabled for a model judged "Too large for this PC" (Recommended, Browse). It becomes enabled, with the warning kept visible beside it and one confirmation naming the size and why it will not run here. Loading such a model stays refused as today.
4. **"Fits beside pictures" mark.** A chat model whose need at the chosen context size, plus the picture model's need (`PictureMemory`, for the checkpoint in use at Standard), fits the card gets a chip on Recommended, Browse and On this PC › Chat models, with one line of explanation behind an ⓘ. Logic in Core with tests; "How fit is judged" in the side panel gains a line. No chip when there is no picture engine or checkpoint.

Checks: real models on a throwaway folder of hard links; graphics memory read before and after Unload everything; a picture drawn in a chat with a marked model (the chat model must not be set aside) and with an unmarked one (it must be).

### M16: Images section (idea 3). Design first

**What is settled already** (follows from M9, not up for choice): it draws through `CardCoordinator`; pictures that are tried stay in memory and only kept ones reach the disk, in the `Pictures` table (so they are encrypted with the lock, in backups, and follow a portable move); checkpoints, LoRAs, VAE and their settings come from `ImageModelCatalog`; prompts are never logged; all three looks, dark and light; a place in the navigation, in Ctrl+K and a colour in Playhouse.

**Controls every design has:** prompt, negative prompt, checkpoint, LoRAs with strengths, sampler, steps, guidance, width and height (with the Tall / Square / Wide shortcuts), seed (the M14 control), hires fix, how many pictures, Draw and Cancel, and for each result: keep, redraw, reuse its settings, save as a file.

**Four layouts to choose from:**

- **A. Classic.** The automatic1111 page: both prompt boxes across the top with Draw beside them; controls bottom left; the picture and a strip of tries bottom right; LoRAs as a fold under the prompts. Most familiar; the busiest screen in the app and the weakest in a narrow window.
- **B. Canvas.** The picture fills the page. The prompt boxes sit at the bottom where the chat box is; every control is in the side panel as folds (Model, LoRAs, Size, Sampling, Seed), which becomes the drawer below 1050 px. Uses the regions every look already has, so it needs the least new styling. Kept pictures are a second tab.
- **C. Sessions.** Like chats: the list holds picture sessions; the page is a feed of what each prompt produced, newest last; the prompt box is at the bottom and controls are in the side panel. Good for working on one idea over many tries; needs a new table for sessions, and decides what a "session" keeps.
- **D. Gallery first.** The section opens on a grid of kept pictures (filter by checkpoint, search the prompt). "New picture" or "Make one like this" opens the workspace (layout B) over it. Best if the point is the collection; one more click before drawing.

Recommended: **B, with D's gallery as its second tab.** It is the automatic1111 controls in this app's own layout, and the only one that works at every window width without new rules.

**To decide before the steps are planned:**

1. The layout (A to D, or a mix).
2. Prompts: typed raw as in automatic1111, or with the tag check offered (a "check my tags" button; never automatic).
3. Whether the rating words from Settings › Pictures are added here, or the prompt is sent exactly as typed.
4. A helper where the chat model turns a sentence into a prompt (the M9 planner), or none.
5. Several pictures per Draw: one after another with the tries strip, or a grid.
6. Kept pictures: only inside DOG.ai (encrypted with the lock), with "Save as a file" per picture, or also a folder DOG.ai writes to.
7. Later extras that would change the layout if wanted: image-to-image, inpainting, upscaling a kept picture.

**Decided by the owner (2026-11-15):**
