- Point 1, the layout: **B, Canvas, with a Kept pictures tab** (chosen from wireframes of the four). Mock-up in the three looks, dark and light, both tabs: `docs/mockups/images-section.html` (open it in a browser; it is a drawing, not app code).
- Points 2 and 3, prompts: sent exactly as typed, as in automatic1111. A "Check my tags" button offers corrections; nothing is changed or added automatically, rating words included.
- Point 4: yes, a prompt helper (the chat model turns a sentence into a prompt the owner can then edit).
- Point 5: yes, several pictures per Draw, one after another into the tries strip.
- Point 6: kept pictures live only inside DOG.ai (the `Pictures` table), with "Save as a file" per picture. No output folder.
- Point 7: upscaling a kept picture is in from the start. Image-to-image and inpainting are not.

**The screen (layout B).** Address `images` (Make) and `images/kept`; a fifth entry in the navigation between Story and Models, in Ctrl+K, on Playhouse's home screen, with its own Playhouse colour (`--c-images`, amber in the mock-up).

- *Make.* The picture fills the page with the strip of tries beside it; under it one line of facts (size, seed, sampler, steps, time) and **Keep**, **Use these settings**, **Upscale…**, **Save as a file…**. The prompt and negative prompt boxes sit where the chat box is, with **Write it for me…** (the prompt helper), **Check my tags**, **Cancel** and **Draw**. The header has the two tabs, the checkpoint button and the side panel button.
- *Side panel* (`AsideRegion`, folds with one-line summaries, a drawer below 1050 px): Model (checkpoint, VAE), LoRAs (tick and strength; a LoRA's trigger words shown as chips that insert into the prompt on a click, never by themselves), Size (Tall / Square / Wide and width, height), Sampling (sampler, scheduler, steps, guidance, clip skip), Seed (the M14 control), Hires fix, Pictures per Draw (1 to 8, `ImageRequest.MaxCount`).
- *Kept pictures.* A grid, newest first, with a box that finds words in the prompt and filters for checkpoint and shape. A picture opens large with **Make one like this** (its settings and seed go to Make), **Upscale…**, **Save as a file…** and **Remove**.

**Rules the steps follow** (defaults chosen here; say so to change one):

- A new control starts from the checkpoint's own settings card and is then remembered (in `InterfaceMemory`'s way: one JSON document of its own, not `AppSettings`). **The prompt boxes are not remembered across a restart**: a prompt reaches the disk only inside a kept picture's row, the M9 rule.
- **Saved prompts, so common ones are not typed again** (owner asked, 2026-11-15; like automatic1111's styles). A saved prompt is a name with a prompt and a negative prompt, kept only because the owner pressed Save: one JSON document in the settings store (inside the database, under the lock, in backups), edited like the chat prompt library. A **Saved prompts** button beside the boxes lists them; one click puts a saved prompt in (**Replace**) or adds it to what is typed (**Add**), and **Save these as…** stores what is in the boxes now. One of them can be marked **Start with this**, and the boxes then start filled with it after a restart. With none marked, the boxes start with the chosen checkpoint's own quality words and negative prompt from its settings card (`QualityBefore`, `QualityAfter`, `Negative`), as visible, editable text. Storage and logic in step 1; the button and list in step 2.
- Kept pictures get a new owner kind (`PictureOwners.Images`) with no one-row limit. The tab lists only these; portraits and story or chat pictures stay where they are.
- The grid needs small previews. They are made in memory and stored in the picture's own row (a new column, so inside the database and under the lock), never as files.
- Nothing is added to what is typed: no quality words, no rating words, no LoRA triggers. `PictureLogFilter.Sending` is still told before every request, and everything draws through `CardCoordinator`.
- Save as a file goes through `IDesktopShell.SaveFileAsync`, so the one-time plain-export note shows while locked.

### M16 steps, and which model to use for each

One step per session, each ending in a build, passing tests, a look at the app, this plan's Status updated and a commit, then the hand-over. M14 and M15 come first.

| # | Step | Delivers | Model / effort |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | **Core and storage** | The raw request (typed values to `ImageRequest`, nothing added), the remembered controls, the `Images` owner kind, the preview column and its migration, listing and searching kept pictures a page at a time. Tests; lock, backup, restore and portable checks for the new column; removed-data check. No screen yet. | Fable 5.2, high |
| 2 | **Make** | The section, navigation, Ctrl+K, Playhouse colour; the Make tab with every control, Draw and Cancel, progress, several pictures per Draw, the tries strip (`PictureTries`), Use these settings, Keep. Real pictures drawn. All three looks, dark and light; the drawer at 700 and 1000 px. | Opus 5.5, high |
| 3 | **Kept pictures** | The grid with previews, find and filters, the large view, Make one like this, Save as a file, Remove. All three looks, dark and light. | Opus 5.5, high |
| 4 | **Helpers** | Write it for me (a DOG.ai model job like the planner: schema, no thinking, answer checked, result put in the box for the owner to edit; the chat model comes back through `EnsureReadyAsync`), Check my tags (`TagList.Current`; offers each change, applies only what is accepted), LoRA trigger chips. | Opus 5.5, high |
| 5 | **Upscale** | A trial first: what the picture engine can do to an existing picture (an upscaler file such as ESRGAN, or only a hires redraw from the seed), and what each costs in graphics memory and time. Then Upscale… on a try and on a kept picture, kept as a new picture beside the original. `PictureMemory` taught the larger sizes. | Fable 5.2, high |
| 6 | **Finish** | Leak check as in M9 step 9 (marker words in prompts and negative prompts, tried and kept, plain and locked), the published portable build, `/code-review high`, every Images screen in three looks dark and light and narrow, `CLAUDE.md` ("How the Images section works"). | Fable 5.2, xhigh |

Why this split: steps 1, 5 and 6 are where a wrong judgement costs most (private pictures and prompts on disk, what the engine can really do, the last look). Steps 2 to 4 reuse what M9 built (the picture maker, `MessagePictures`, the planner, the tag list).

### Sessions, and which model to use (M14 to M16)

| # | Milestone | Model / effort |
|---|---|---|
| M14 | Quick fixes | Opus 5.5, high |
| M15 | Graphics memory and downloads | Fable 5.2, high |
| M16 | Images section | Six steps, each with its own; see "M16 steps" |

Why: M14 repeats patterns the code has. M15 changes who holds the graphics card and when, where a wrong judgement leaves a model stuck or a picture waiting.

## M10 to M13: A tidier interface (planned 2026-11-14; all four done 2026-11-14)

**Why.** Every milestone added controls and none took any away. A review of the running app (Workbench dark, an empty data folder, window 1400 × 860; the other looks and chats with content were read in code only) found:

| Screen | Height against the window | Controls on the page |
|---|---|---|
| Settings | 2.5 screens | 30 |
| Models, Images | 2.5 screens | 28 |
| New character | 2.3 screens | 30 |
| New story | 1.6 screens | 22 |

Also: the Models side panel shows the same five panels on all seven tabs; hardware is shown twice on Recommended; a character chat stacks about seven always-open side panels; nearly every setting carries a two- or three-line paragraph; the picture checkpoint is chosen in two places (Settings and Models, Images).

**The ten ideas**, numbered as they were put to the owner:

1. A default model per job: one block of dropdowns (General, Characters, Story, Document search, Pictures), each with "No default" ("Pick automatically" for document search), in place of the "Default for…" menu on every model.
2. Settings in categories (Appearance, Chats and stories, Voice, Pictures, Privacy, Windows, Backup, About) with a side list and a search box.
3. The Models side panel shows only what fits the tab; hardware once; one Connection spot for the online note and the Hugging Face token.
4. Shorter help text: one line under a control, the rest behind an info button.
5. Model cards with one main button (Load); Benchmark, Delete and the file path in a "⋯" menu.
6. Collapsible chat side panels with a one-line summary each ("Style · Balanced"), remembered, and a button to hide the whole panel.
7. Character and story editors in tabs (Basics, Personality and scenario, Messages, Looks and voice, Advanced) with a Save bar that stays visible.
8. Models, Images split up: Checkpoints, LoRAs, Tags, and the test picture folded away as a diagnostic.
9. Hide what cannot work yet: the composer's microphone, hands-free and read-aloud buttons appear once a voice model exists.
10. Models regrouped (Get models; On this PC; Engines) with downloads as a tray button in the header; and Ctrl+K to jump to any chat, character, story or setting.

### M10: Defaults and Settings (ideas 1, 2, 4 for Settings, 5)

1. **Shared pieces first.** A collapsible section that remembers open or closed, a "⋯" menu, an info button for long help text, and page tabs. Built once and checked in every look, so later steps only reuse them. What is remembered (which sections are closed, the last Settings category) is kept in the settings store, with its logic in Core so it can be tested.
2. **Default model per job.** The block at the top of Models, Library. It writes the same `AppSettings.DefaultModels` the menus write today; the Pictures row writes `ImageModelCatalog.Chosen`. Each model keeps a chip saying what it is the default for.
3. **Model cards.** Load stays; the rest moves into "⋯".
4. **Settings in categories**, with search and trimmed help text. The ids the walkthroughs use must keep working.

### M11: Models section (ideas 3, 8, and the regrouping and tray of 10)

1. Side panel per tab; hardware once; the Connection spot.
2. Three groups in place of seven tabs. Old addresses (`models/images` and the rest) keep working.
3. Downloads tray in the header.
4. Images split into sub-tabs.

The riskiest milestone: about 49 messages and links in 35 files name a place such as "Models › Images", some of them error messages in Core and Engine. Each is reworded when its target moves, and `CLAUDE.md` with them.

### M12: Chats and editors (ideas 6, 7, 9)

1. Collapsible side panels and the hide button, in General, Characters, group chats and Story.
2. Voice buttons only when a voice model exists.
3. Editors in tabs with a Save bar. All tabs stay in one form (hidden, not removed), so switching tabs never loses typing; a problem found on saving opens the tab it is on.

### M13: Quick-jump and final pass (Ctrl+K of idea 10, the rest of 4)

1. Ctrl+K, built on the existing Search: an index in Core with tests; the box sits inside `.shell`; the key is caught in the page and in `MainWindow`, as Ctrl+L is.
2. Help text trimmed on the screens not yet touched.
3. Every screen looked at in three looks, dark and light; the portable build; `CLAUDE.md` brought up to date.

### Sessions, and which model to use for each

One milestone per session. Each ends in a build, passing tests, a look at the app in three looks (dark and light), this plan's Status updated, and a commit. Then the session stops and hands over (owner's instruction, 2026-11-14; the rule is in `CLAUDE.md`): it tells the owner to start a new session, names the model and effort from this table, and gives the prompt to paste into it. "Effort" is the session's effort setting.

| # | Milestone | Model / effort |
|---|---|---|
| M10 | Defaults and Settings | Opus 5.5, high |
| M11 | Models section | Fable 5.2, high |
| M12 | Chats and editors | Opus 5.5, high |
| M13 | Quick-jump and final pass | Fable 5.2, high |

Why this split: M11 moves things that about 49 messages and links point at, next to the only code that goes online, and M13 is the last look at everything, so a wrong judgement costs most there. M10 and M12 mostly repeat patterns the code already has (the Models side list, `Modal`, the existing panels and editors), so Opus 5.5 is enough and cheaper.

### For the owner to decide (asked 2026-11-14)

- **Where the picture engine's install lives. Decided (owner, 2026-11-14): under Engines**, beside the chat engine. Done in M11; the picture tabs show a note with a link while it is missing.
- **Where the context size is set. Decided (owner, 2026-11-14): in the defaults block.** Done in M10: Models → Library → Default models; the side panel only says which size the fit badges use.
- **Pictures with nothing chosen. Decided (owner, 2026-11-14): "Pick automatically"**, like Document search: DOG.ai draws with the first checkpoint found, as before.

## M9: Pictures (planned 2026-11-13; built 2026-11-13 and 14, all nine steps done)

DOG.ai draws pictures on this PC with **stable-diffusion.cpp**, a sibling of llama.cpp, run the same way as the chat engine: a child process on `127.0.0.1` that DOG.ai starts and stops. Nothing goes online to draw.

**Decisions the owner made (2026-11-13):**
- stable-diffusion.cpp as a second engine (not ComfyUI, not ONNX, not an online service).
- First version supports the **SDXL family only** (Illustrious, NoobAI, Pony, base SDXL): one checkpoint file each, plus LoRAs.
- Pictures are made in two places: **character and persona portraits** and **story illustrations**. A picture button in chats and a standalone Images screen are later steps.
- **Civitai files are added by hand** (downloaded in a browser, dropped into the image models folder). **Hugging Face downloads happen in the app**, as for chat models. Civitai is not added to the allow-list.
- After the chat model writes a picture prompt, DOG.ai **draws at once** by default; a switch in Settings makes it **show the prompt first**.
- The owner supplies an Illustrious checkpoint and LoRAs for the trial.
- LoRAs can be **character LoRAs as well as style LoRAs**: a card can carry its own, and a checkpoint can have style LoRAs that apply to every picture.
- Default shapes: **Tall** for portraits, **Wide** for story illustrations.
- **No automatic illustrations**; pictures are made only when asked for.
- A story picture shows **at most two or three people**.
- Prompt wording follows the checkpoint, with no switch to flip per picture: **Danbooru-style tags** for Illustrious, NoobAI and Pony, sentences for base SDXL. The checkpoint's quality words ("masterpiece, best quality…", Pony's score tags) and negative prompt are added by DOG.ai, never left to the chat model.

**What was read about the engine (2026-11-13, from its documentation, not yet run here):**
- It ships a server program, `sd-server.exe`, with its own API: start a picture (returns a job), ask a job for its progress and a small preview, cancel a job, list what it can do, load and unload a model without exiting. Pictures come back in the reply, not as files; PNG, JPEG or WebP.
- LoRAs are given per picture as a list (file and strength). `<lora:...>` tags typed in a prompt are **not** read by the server, so DOG.ai picks LoRAs in its own controls.
- Per picture: clip skip, sampler, steps, "hires fix" (draw, enlarge, refine). Per loaded model: the VAE. A face-detail pass is mentioned but not documented. V-prediction is not in the server's documentation: **the trial must settle it.**
- Windows downloads are a CUDA 12 zip plus a separate zip of NVIDIA runtime files. MIT licence.
- The server has a built-in web page. It will listen only on `127.0.0.1` on a random port, like llama-server.

### Trial results (step 1, 2026-11-13)

Run by hand on this PC, outside DOG.ai: engine build `master-1094-3bad892` (CUDA 12 zip plus the NVIDIA runtime zip, both SHA-256 checked against the release page; about 1.2 GB unpacked, most of it NVIDIA libraries), the owner's checkpoint `illustrious_novaFurryXL_ilV180A` (6.6 GB, recognised as SDXL, eps-prediction) and three LoRAs (detail, style, character; all plain LoRAs of 218 MB). 25 pictures drawn; seven looked at.

- **It works, and the pictures are good.** The first try (tags: silver hair, green eyes, scar on cheek, leather armor, green cloak, forest, dappled sunlight) showed every tag, with no black-picture VAE problem and no extra VAE file needed.
- **Speed (832×1216):** Draft (20 steps) 5 s; Standard (28 steps) 6.5 s; Fine (28 steps, hires fix ×1.5 with 14 more steps) 14 to 15 s, giving 1248×1824. About 5 steps a second. The very first picture after a start takes about 4 s longer (the model is read into graphics memory then, not at start, so the server itself is up in under a second). Four pictures in one request: 25 s.
- **Graphics memory (above what Windows already uses):** about 8 GB held while the model is loaded and idle, about 11.5 GB at the peak of a Standard picture, about 17 GB at the peak of a Fine one. Unloading through the API gives it all back without stopping the server.
- **Beside the chat model: no.** The owner's 27B chat model is a 16.8 GB file, so the two cannot both be on the card; this is arithmetic, not a test (the test was not run). The swap in "Sharing the graphics card" is needed every time with this chat model, and Fine quality needs the card nearly to itself. *(Step 3 ran it: the 27B holds 17 GB and is set aside for every picture; a 7 GB chat model stays loaded beside the picture model, the card peaking at 20.1 GB.)*
- **LoRAs:** each worked alone and all three together. The character LoRA with its trigger word gave the same character in two shapes and seeds; without the LoRA the same prompt gave a generic one. Changing the LoRA set costs about 3 s on the next picture (about 10 s for three at once); going back to none also works. Trigger words and the most common training tags can be read from the files' own metadata.
- **Cancel** stopped a 60-step picture within about 0.3 s, and the next picture was normal. **Preview** ("proj" mode needs no extra file) gave a new small picture every few steps. Progress reports the stage (conditioning, sampling, decoding, encoding) and the step.
- **Hires fix** makes a visibly sharper picture but can change small things (it drew a second scar). So Fine is for a picture already chosen, not for trying ideas.
- **Picture size on disk:** PNG about 1.7 MB (3.6 MB for Fine), JPEG at 92 about 255 KB, WebP at 90 about 128 KB. Decision for storage: **WebP**, with the quality number fixed in step 5 after comparing by eye (not compared closely yet). *(Step 5 compared them: quality 90; see Status.)*
- **Privacy:** the server wrote **no file** to its working folder or its temp folder in the whole run, and at its normal log level the prompt does **not** appear in its output (model path, timings and step counters do). Its verbose level was not tried and must never be switched on by DOG.ai. `--disable-image-metadata` exists, so the prompt need not be embedded in a picture that gets exported.
- **V-prediction:** the engine has a load option for it (`--prediction v`). Not tried: the owner's checkpoint is the normal kind. Needs a v-prediction checkpoint to confirm.
- **More than planned is there:** a face-detail pass (needs one small extra detection model), reference-picture control for SDXL (IP-Adapter), image-to-image and inpainting. None are in M9, but they are no longer blocked by the engine.
- **Not tried:** drawing while a chat model is loaded; loading a different checkpoint through the API (only unloading was tried; the first load was from the command line); a separate VAE file; LyCORIS-type LoRAs (none in the samples); face detail; verbose logging; the engine under DOG.ai's portable temp folder.
- **Suggested starting numbers for this family** (tuned in step 6: Euler instead of Euler a, the rest stayed; see Status): Euler a, guidance 5.5, clip skip 2, quality words "masterpiece, best quality, amazing quality, very aesthetic, absurdres", negative "worst quality, low quality, bad quality, lowres, bad anatomy, bad hands, jpeg artifacts, signature, watermark, text".

### How it will work

**Engine (`DogAI.Engine/Images`).** `ImageServer` follows `EmbeddingServer`: started the first time a picture is asked for, random loopback port, `EngineEnvironment.Clean`, `ProcessJob` so it dies with DOG.ai, stopped when idle. It is found by its own locator in `data\engine` (override `DOGAI_SD_SERVER` for tests), and Models' engine updater gets a second entry that downloads it from the project's GitHub releases (one new allowed path in `OriginPolicy`, checksum checked). *Built in step 2: it lives in `data\engine\images`; no `OriginPolicy` change was needed (GitHub's hosts were already allowed); a checkpoint is loaded by starting the program with it, because the API's load works only inside the server's own folder layout (see Status).* Core sees only an `IImageEngine` interface, so tests use a fake.

**Sharing the graphics card.** A 27B chat model and an SDXL model do not fit in 24 GB together. A small coordinator decides per picture: if both fit (measured free memory), keep both; otherwise the prompt is written first, the chat model is unloaded, the picture is drawn, and the chat model is loaded again **only when it is next needed** (not straight away, so "Try again" and a run of pictures cost one swap, not one each). The image model is let go after a short idle time. A chat model the owner unloaded by hand stays unloaded. *Built in step 3: the coordinator is the picture engine the app hands out; the chat model is "set aside" rather than unloaded, so chat pages keep working and the next message loads it again; a chat request during a picture waits for it; the picture model's idle time is 2 minutes (see Status).*

**Image models folder.** `<models>\image\checkpoints`, `\loras`, `\vae`, `\upscalers`. Only `.safetensors` is accepted; the old `.ckpt` format can carry program code and is refused with a plain message. DOG.ai reads each file's header to tell what it is (SDXL checkpoint, SDXL LoRA, something else) and shows files it cannot use as such.

**Models → Images tab.** Installed checkpoints and LoRAs, **Open folder** (for Civitai files), recommended downloads from Hugging Face, the image engine's install and update. Each checkpoint has a small settings card so pictures come out right without fiddling: prompt style (tags or sentences), the "quality" words it wants before and after a prompt, the negative prompt, sampler, steps, guidance, clip skip, normal or v-prediction, and its VAE. Known families get these filled in when the file is recognised; the owner can change them. Each LoRA has its trigger words (read from the file when it has them) and a default strength.

**Looks on a card.** Characters and personas get a **Looks** box: appearance tags ("silver hair, green eyes, scar on left cheek…"), and optionally a LoRA with its strength. The chat model can draft the tags from the card's description. Stored in the card's extra data so export and import keep it. This is what keeps a character recognisable from one picture to the next. *Built in step 6: in the card's extensions for characters, a column for personas; a checkpoint's style LoRAs are tick boxes in its settings (see Status).*

**Writing the prompt (`DogAI.Core/Images`).** A DOG.ai model job (schema-constrained, checked before use, thinking off) turns a card or a story passage into the pieces of a prompt in the checkpoint's style. The final prompt is assembled by code, not by the model: checkpoint quality words + the Looks of the people in the scene + scene tags + the checkpoint's negative prompt. For a story, the people are the passage's cast (`StoryPrompt.People`), at most two or three (SDXL handles more badly, and the plan says so in the app).

*Tag prompts.* For tag-style checkpoints the model does not write a free prompt. It first picks **one moment** of the passage that can be shown as a still picture, then fills named slots: who is in it (as a count, "1girl, 1boy"), clothing, expression, pose or action, place, time and light, framing ("upper body", "from above"). Code puts the slots in the order these checkpoints expect and adds each person's Looks from their card (so the model cannot change a character's hair colour). Every tag is checked against a **bundled list of real Danbooru tags** (shipped with DOG.ai, no lookup online): near misses are corrected to the real tag, unknown ones are dropped, and the prompt is kept short. Where the list comes from and its licence are settled in the trial. What this does well: one or two people, their looks, clothes, mood, place and light. What it does poorly, said plainly in the app: exact actions between people, one person's features leaking onto another, unusual objects, anything that depends on what happened earlier in the story. *Built in step 6: the list is the general and species tags of Danbooru and e621 (the owner chose the merged file), 33,057 tags from the a1111-sd-webui-tagcomplete project (MIT); framing is one of six sizes and one of five views (see Status).*

**Quality choices.** Three named levels in place of raw numbers: **Draft** (fast, for trying ideas), **Standard**, **Fine** (hires fix, and face detail if the trial shows it works). Shapes: Tall, Square, Wide, using SDXL's native sizes. A live preview and step count while drawing; **Cancel** works mid-picture. Every picture keeps its seed and settings, so **Edit and redraw** changes one thing and keeps the rest. Raw settings stay available behind "More". *Tuned in step 6: Draft is 20 of 28 steps, Fine enlarges 1.5 times with Lanczos and refines at strength 0.3, and Illustrious draws with Euler so that a Draft shows what the Standard picture will be. Face detail was not tried (it needs an extra model). The live preview, Cancel and Edit and redraw are the picture maker's (step 7); the levels and shapes are ready for it.*

**Portraits.** **Generate picture** in the character and persona editors opens the picture maker (shared component in `Components/Images/`): Draw, Try again (variations side by side, held in memory), Edit and redraw, **Use this picture**, which hands the result to the existing cropper. Nothing is saved until Use. *Built in step 7: Try again keeps the plan and takes a new seed (no chat model job); Edit and redraw changes the prompt, quality, shape or LoRAs and keeps the seed; the picture is kept (a `Pictures` row) only when the character or persona is saved, one per owner (see Status).*

**Story illustrations.** **Illustrate** in a passage's actions. The picture sits above the passage, opens large on click, and can be redrawn or removed. Which picture belongs to a passage is recorded through `StoryTurn`. HTML and EPUB exports include the pictures; Markdown does not. *Built in step 8: kept at Use (the passage already exists), one per passage; the EPUB carries JPEG copies, the web page the WebP itself (see Status).*

**Keeping pictures (and keeping them private).** Kept pictures go **inside the database** (a new `Pictures` table), so the lock, backups, restore and portable mode cover them with no new machinery. They are stored compressed (the trial compares PNG and WebP for size and looks); tried-and-discarded variations never touch the disk. The image engine is started so that it writes no picture, prompt or preview to disk, and DOG.ai's log never records a prompt (the engine prints prompts in its own output, so those lines are filtered). Drawing counts as "busy" for the idle lock. *Built in step 5: WebP at quality 90, made by the engine itself; the engine's own lines reach the log through a filter (it prints no prompt at its normal level, and the filter holds if a later version does); see Status.*

**Settings → Pictures.** Default checkpoint, default quality, "Show me the prompt before drawing" (off by default), and the **rating** pictures aim for (owner's decision 2026-11-13: a setting, not the chat model's choice). The rating tag is added by code, the tags for anything above the chosen rating go into the negative prompt, and the prompt job is told the rating so it picks a moment that fits. Which rating words each family uses (Illustrious, NoobAI, Pony differ) sits on the checkpoint's settings card. *Built in step 7: four levels; the settings live in the `image-models` document (see Status).*

### Steps, and which model to use for each

Each step is one session ending in a build, passing tests, a look at the app, and a commit. "Effort" is the session's effort setting.

| # | Step | Delivers | Model / effort |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 ✅ | **Trial** (no app code; results above) | The engine downloaded to a scratch folder and run by hand with the owner's checkpoint and LoRAs. Measured: load time, seconds per picture at each quality level, memory used, whether it fits beside the chat model, v-prediction, VAE, hires fix, face detail, LoRA formats, cancel, unload, PNG against WebP, and what the engine writes to disk and to its output. Ends with the open points in this plan settled and the three quality levels given real numbers. | Fable 5.2, high |
| 2 ✅ | **Engine** | Locator, download and update in Models, `ImageServer`, the API client, `IImageEngine`, tests with a fake and one real-engine smoke test behind an environment variable. | Opus 5.5, high |
| 3 ✅ | **Sharing the graphics card** | The coordinator between chat model and image model, with lazy reload; busy state for the idle lock. | Fable 5.2, high |
| 4 ✅ | **Models → Images** | Folders, file recognition, the Images tab, checkpoint and LoRA settings cards, Hugging Face downloads, Open folder. All three looks, dark and light. | Opus 5.5, medium |
| 5 ✅ | **Keeping pictures** | `Pictures` table and migration, lock, backup, restore and portable checks, log filtering, a first leak check. | Fable 5.2, high |
| 6 ✅ | **Prompts and quality** | Looks on cards, the prompt job, prompt assembly, quality levels tuned by looking at real pictures from the owner's checkpoint. | Fable 5.2, high |
| 7 ✅ | **Portraits** | The picture maker and Generate picture in both editors, Settings → Pictures. All three looks, dark and light. | Opus 5.5, high |
| 8 ✅ | **Story illustrations** | Illustrate on passages, the large view, exports. All three looks, dark and light. | Opus 5.5, high |
| 9 ✅ | **Finish** | Full leak check as in M8, walkthroughs in the published portable build, `/code-review high`, this plan's Status and `CLAUDE.md` ("How pictures work") updated. | Fable 5.2, xhigh |

Why this split: steps 1, 3, 5, 6 and 9 are where a wrong judgement costs most (what the engine can really do, two models fighting over memory, private data on disk, whether pictures look good), so they get the strongest model. Steps 2, 4, 7 and 8 mostly repeat patterns the code already has (`EmbeddingServer`, the Voice tab, the card editor), so Opus 5.5 is enough and cheaper. Sonnet and Haiku are not suggested for any step: every one of them touches several projects at once.

### Left for later (not in M9)

A picture button in General chats (character and group chats got theirs on 2026-11-14, see Status; General's is planned in M17 step 5); a standalone Images screen with a gallery; newer model families (Flux, Z-Image, Qwen-Image); changing an existing picture (image-to-image, inpainting); pose and reference-picture control; automatic illustration of every passage or chapter; in-app Civitai downloads; video.

## M8: Locked data (built 2026-11-13)

Part 1 (the machinery and a plain lock screen) and part 2 (the screens, locking, the leak checks) are recorded in Status at the top, with what was checked. Where building it changed or settled something in the plan below, the text says so in *italics*.

**Goal.** Without the password, nobody can read anything DOG.ai keeps: not on this PC, not from a copied data folder, not from a backup zip, not from a lost USB stick holding a portable copy. It is off by default and turned on in Settings.

**What it does not protect against, said plainly.** Malware running as the owner while DOG.ai is unlocked; someone at the PC while it is unlocked; Windows' own traces (listed under Portable mode above). Model files are not encrypted: they are public downloads and say nothing about the owner.

### How it works for the owner

- **Turning it on** (Settings → Privacy → Lock DOG.ai with a password): choose a password, then DOG.ai shows a **recovery key** (24 letters and digits in groups) to print or write down, and asks for part of it back before going on. The database is then encrypted in place; this takes seconds to a minute.
- **Starting DOG.ai** shows only a lock screen: the password box, **Use Windows Hello** if it is set up on this PC, and **I forgot my password** (asks for the recovery key, then a new password). Nothing else loads until it is unlocked: no chats, no tray menu, no Ctrl+Alt+Space, no default model.
- **Windows Hello** (face, fingerprint or Windows PIN) is offered after the first unlock on each PC. It is per PC: a portable copy on another PC asks for the password once, then offers Hello there too.
- **Locking:** **Lock now** (Settings, the tray menu, Ctrl+L), and after an idle time chosen in Settings (off, 5, 15, 30, 60 minutes; default 15). Locking restarts DOG.ai into the lock screen (`IDesktopShell.Restart`), which is the only sure way to clear the key and every decrypted chat from memory. A loaded model is unloaded by that restart; the default model loads again after unlocking.
- **Wrong passwords:** after five wrong tries in a row, each further try waits longer (30 s, then 1 min, then 5 min). Nothing is ever wiped.
- **Changing the password** is instant (only the key wrapping changes, not the database). **A new recovery key** can be made at any time; the old one stops working.
- **Turning it off** asks for the password and decrypts the database back to a plain file.
- **Forgotten password and lost recovery key:** the data is gone for good. The setup screen says this in one plain sentence, and so does the "forgot" screen.

### How it works inside

- **Library:** SQLite3 Multiple Ciphers (MIT licence) through its SQLitePCLRaw bundle, in place of the plain `e_sqlite3` bundle EF Core uses now; the key is passed through the connection string's `Password`. The default cipher (ChaCha20-Poly1305) is used. To be checked first, before any other work: that its bundle matches the SQLitePCLRaw version EF Core 9 uses, that FTS5 (`MessageSearch`) works, and that every existing test passes on it unchanged. If any of these fails, stop and report rather than switch libraries silently. *Outcome: the bundle is `SQLite3MC.PCLRaw.bundle` 2.6.1 (needs SQLitePCLRaw 3.0.2), and Microsoft.Data.Sqlite is raised to 10.0.15 because 9.0.23 cannot reopen an encrypted database through `Password`; the owner approved both. FTS5 works and every existing test passes. The key is passed as a raw key (`raw:` plus 64 hex digits), which skips the library's own password stretching on every open.*
- **Keys:** a random 256-bit database key. It is never stored as it is; it is stored wrapped (AES-GCM) three ways in a small plain file beside the database, `lock.json` (it must be outside the database, because it is read before the database can be opened):
  - by a key derived from the password (PBKDF2-SHA256, 600,000 rounds, random salt; built into .NET, no new package),
  - by a key derived from the recovery key, the same way,
  - by Windows Hello, one entry per PC: a Hello key (`KeyCredentialManager`) signs a fixed challenge, and the signature is turned into a wrapping key. Hello keys are RSA with PKCS#1 v1.5 signatures, which are the same every time for the same challenge, so this works without storing any secret; Windows asks for the face, fingerprint or PIN each time. To be confirmed on this PC at the start of M8; if the signatures differ, Hello falls back to a DPAPI-wrapped key released after a Hello check (weaker: anything running as the owner could unwrap it), and the plan is brought back to the owner before going on. *Confirmed on this PC: same bytes every time; the fallback is not used. The signature goes through HKDF-SHA256 with a per-entry salt to make the wrapping key. One Hello key ("DOG.ai data lock") serves every DOG.ai data folder in a Windows account; each folder has its own challenge.*
  - `lock.json` holds salts, round counts and wrapped keys only; nothing in it opens the database without the password, the recovery key or the owner's Hello.
- **Core** (`DogAI.Core/Privacy`): `LockFile` (read, write, wrap, unwrap, change password, new recovery key), `RecoveryKey` (make, format, read back with spaces and dashes forgiven), `UnlockThrottle` (wait times). All testable without Windows Hello; Hello sits behind an interface implemented in App.
- **Start-up order** in `App.OnStartup`: pending restore → portable move → **unlock** → anything that opens the database. Settings live in the database, so the lock screen's look (skin, dark or light) is copied into `lock.json` when it changes, so the lock screen matches the app.
- **Turning it on and off:** the database is copied into a new encrypted (or plain) file (the library's own copy-with-a-new-key, or `PRAGMA rekey`; which one is settled in the first check), checked by opening it and counting rows in every table, then swapped in; the old file and its `-wal`/`-shm` are deleted. On an SSD, deleted data cannot be guaranteed gone; the help text says so and suggests turning lock on early. *Settled: copy with a new key (`VACUUM INTO`). It happens only at start, at the lock screen, once the password has been entered: turning the lock on in Settings writes `lock.json` and restarts; the database is encrypted when the new password is typed at the lock screen. Turning it off is likewise carried out at the next unlock.*
- **Backups:** an encrypted database gives an encrypted copy, and the backup zip gets `lock.json` too, so a backup opens with the same password or recovery key on any PC (Hello entries are dropped from the zip; they only work on their own PC). The backup label `"DOG AI"` and the `dogai.db` entry name stay as they are. **Restore** must accept all four cases (plain backup into plain or locked DOG.ai; locked backup into plain or locked DOG.ai): a locked backup asks for its own password before staging. Restoring a plain backup into a locked DOG.ai encrypts it while applying, so no plain copy is left behind. `lock.json` is not a machine setting (it travels with the data), but the Hello entries are. *Built: a locked backup restored into a locked DOG.ai is re-encrypted under this DOG.ai's key, so the current password stays; restored into a plain DOG.ai it brings its own lock. The swap at start needs no key; this PC's settings are carried over after the unlock (`DatabaseBackup.FinishRestore`).*
- **Portable mode:** `lock.json` sits in `data\`, so a moved or copied portable folder still opens with the password. `PortableMove` runs after unlock, since it rewrites paths stored in the database.

### Other places text could be written, to check and close

The database holds everything the owner makes (chats, cards, stories, documents' text and vectors, settings), so encrypting it covers almost everything. These are the rest, each to be checked during M8 and listed in Status afterwards as closed or as left:

- **Logs** (`data\logs`): must never hold message, card, document or story text. A quick search finds no logging of message text today; a test will check by running a chat with a marker word and searching the log file.
- **WebView2 folder** (`data\webview`): Blazor Hybrid serves pages from inside the app, so chat text should not reach its cache, but this must be checked by searching the folder for a marker word after a session. If text is found there, the folder is cleared on every lock and exit.
- **Temp** (`data\temp` and the system temp): Whisper, sherpa-onnx and llama-server scratch files; checked the same way, with dictation and reading aloud used during the session.
- **llama-server:** must not be started with any option that saves prompts or slots to disk (checked in `EngineManager`).
- **Exports** (markdown, card files, story exports) are files the owner chooses to write and are left as plain files; the save dialog for a locked DOG.ai says so once.
- **Clipboard:** copied text is the owner's choice; not handled.

### Tests and checks

- Unit tests: wrap and unwrap with password, recovery key and a fake Hello; wrong password refused; password change keeps the same database key; old recovery key refused after a new one; `lock.json` round-trip and refusal of a damaged file; throttle times.
- Data tests: a plain database encrypted and decrypted back with all rows intact (using an M7 database and a current one); the encrypted file holds no marker word in its bytes; search (FTS5) works on an encrypted database; all four restore cases; a backup of a locked database holds no plain copy.
- In the app, on a throwaway data folder: turn lock on, restart, unlock with password, with recovery key, with Windows Hello (needs the owner at the PC for face or fingerprint; the Windows PIN works too); lock now; idle lock; tray and Ctrl+Alt+Space dead while locked; turn lock off. Lock screen, setup and recovery screens looked at in all three looks, dark and light. A portable copy moved to another folder still unlocks.
- The marker-word search of the whole data folder after a session with chat, a document, dictation and reading aloud.

### Decided by the owner (2026-11-13)

1. Idle lock defaults to 15 minutes.
2. **Lock now** works only inside DOG.ai (Settings, tray menu, Ctrl+L); no global key.
3. Every lock unloads the model; it reloads after unlocking.

## Verification

- `dotnet build` and `dotnet test` clean at every milestone; solution opens and F5-runs in VS 2022.
- **Unit tests:** card PNG/JSON round-trip (V2 and V3), GGUF header parser, fit estimator, prompt builder and context budgeter, lorebook matching, message-tree branching, CYOA schema parsing, download resume and hash check against a local test server.
- **Engine smoke test:** load a small GGUF, stream a reply, confirm GPU offload and VRAM release on unload.
- **Offline test:** with the Online switch off, exercise all three modes and confirm the only sockets opened are loopback; then confirm with the network adapter disabled that everything outside the Models section works.
- **Manual walkthrough per milestone** in the running app (I'll drive it and report what I could and could not verify).

## Model note

Fable is worth keeping for M0–M1, where the architecture gets set. From M2 onward most of the work is routine implementation against that architecture, and Sonnet 5.5 or Opus 5.5 would handle it at lower usage; I'll flag any milestone where the harder reasoning is worth switching back for (M4's story engine is the likely one).
